.∎

5.3.
When
G
is of type C

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be of type
\text{C}_{n}
where
n\geqslant 2
, and let
\gamma=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\epsilon_{i}\in Q^{\vee}
. Then (using notation from Definition
4.14
and (
4
)) in
\operatorname{Coh}^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e})
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(a_{i}x_{i}-a_{i+1}x_{i+1})

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(a_{1}+\cdots-a_{n},a_{1}+\cdots+a_{n-1}+a_{n})=\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}+\cdots a_{n-1}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-a_{n},a_{n}),

where as in Definition
4.14
,
x_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{x_{i}^{+},x_{i}^{-}\}
is a divisor on
\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.5
, the underlying line bundles of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(\gamma)
is
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}\sqcup\mathbf{P}^{1}}(a_{i}-a_{i+1})
, and the underlying bundle of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}
is
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(2a_{n})
.

Moreover, using the notation in Lemma
4.13
,
z\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
acts on the stalk of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}(\gamma)
at
x_{j}^{\pm}
as
z^{\mp a_{j}}
. Thus, by
[
BKK24
, Lemma 6.2]
, we know

{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(\gamma)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(a_{i}x_{i}-a_{i+1}x_{i+1}).

Similarly, we conclude from Corollary
2.11
that
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(\gamma)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(a_{n},-a_{n})
.
∎

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be of type
\text{C}_{n}
where
n\geqslant 2
. Then for
k>0
,

\displaystyle C_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}

\displaystyle=-[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]

\displaystyle C_{w_{k\epsilon_{i}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]&k=1,i=1\\
-[\xi_{k-1}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]&k>1,i=1\\
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i-1}}}((k-1)x_{i-1}-kx_{i})]&1<i\leqslant n,\end{cases}

\displaystyle C_{w_{-k\epsilon_{i}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-kx_{i}+(k-1)x_{i+1})]&1\leqslant i<n\\
[\xi_{k-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)]&i=n.\end{cases}

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the Weyl group ordering, as described in Lemma
3.7
. By Corollary
2.25
, we have
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1})]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]
, so
C_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}}=-[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]
. Note that

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{2})]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(\epsilon_{2})]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{2})],

so
C_{w_{\epsilon_{2}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{2})]
. Moreover, by inductively applying Lemma
2.29
and Lemma
5.11
, we obtain:

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{n})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{n-1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{n-1}}}\!(-x_{n})]

\displaystyle=\cdots

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})],

so

(49)

[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})].

Thus,

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{3})]

\displaystyle=\cdots

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{n})]-\sum_{i=2}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1})]-[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-\sum_{i=2}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{2})],

where for the last equality we used (
49
) tensored with
[\operatorname{sgn}]\in K^{\mathbb{G}_{m}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2}(*)
. Thus
C_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}=[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]
.

For
k>0
and
i>1
, again by repeated application of Lemma
2.29
and Lemma
5.11
,

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(k\epsilon_{i})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{i-1}+(k-1)\epsilon_{i})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i-1}}}(-kx_{i})]

\displaystyle=\cdots

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(k\epsilon_{i-1})]-\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i-1}}}(jx_{i-1}-(k-j)x_{i})].

Now note the trick:

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i-1}}}(jx_{i-1}-(k-j)x_{i})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i-1}}}(jx_{i-1}-(k-j)x_{i})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i-1}}}((k-j-1)x_{i-1}-(j+1)x_{i})]\big)

(50)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i-1}}}(jx_{i-1}-(j+1)x_{i})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i-1}}}((k-j-1)x_{i-1}-(k-j)x_{i})]\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i-1}}}(jx_{i-1}-(j+1)x_{i})],

so by induction on the Bruhat order we conclude that
C_{w_{k\epsilon_{i}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i-1}}}((k-1)x_{i-1}-kx_{i})]
.

Next, we calculate
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-k\epsilon_{i})]
where
i<n
. By a repeated application of Lemma
2.29
and Lemma
5.11
, we have:

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-k\epsilon_{i})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-(k-1)\epsilon_{i}-\epsilon_{i+1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-kx_{i})]

\displaystyle=\cdots

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-k\epsilon_{i+1})]+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-(k-j)x_{i}+jx_{i+1})],

where as in (
50
),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-(k-j)x_{i}+jx_{i+1})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-(k-j)x_{i}+jx_{i+1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-(j+1)x_{i}+(k-j-1)x_{i+1})]\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-(k-j)x_{i}+(k-j-1)x_{i+1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-(j+1)x_{i}+jx_{i+1})]\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-(j+1)x_{i}+jx_{i+1})].

Thus,
C_{w_{-k\epsilon_{i}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-kx_{i}+(k-1)x_{i+1})]
, as desired.

Next, we calculate
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-k\epsilon_{n})]
. By a repeated application of Lemma
2.29
and Lemma
5.11
and Remark
2.10
, we have:

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-k\epsilon_{n})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-(k-1)\epsilon_{n})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(k,-k)]

\displaystyle=\cdots

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(k\epsilon_{n})]+\sum_{j=1-k}^{k}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(j,-j)].

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(k\epsilon_{n})]+\sum_{j=1}^{k}[\xi_{j-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)],

where the last equality uses the observation:

\sum_{j=1-k}^{k}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(j,-j)]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1-k,-k)]\otimes\sum_{j=0}^{2k-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(j,2k-1-j)]=\sum_{j=1}^{k}[\xi_{j-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)]

by Lemma
2.9
. Thus
C_{w_{-k\epsilon_{n}}}=[\xi_{k-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-1,0)]
.

Next, for elements of the form
k\epsilon_{1}
with
k>0
, by a repeated application of Lemma
2.29
and Lemma
5.11
,

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(k\epsilon_{1})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}((k-1)\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}((k-1)x_{1}-x_{2})]

\displaystyle=\cdots

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}((k-1)\epsilon_{1})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}((k-1)x_{1}-x_{2})]-[\operatorname{sgn}^{k-1}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-\sum_{i=2}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}((k-1)\epsilon_{1})]-[\operatorname{sgn}^{k-1}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{2})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\big((k-1)x_{1}-x_{2}\big)]\big),

where the last equality used (
49
). Now note the
{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}
-equivariant exact sequences:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})\rightarrow

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}((k-1)x_{i}-x_{i+1})\rightarrow\sum_{m=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{i}}\langle m\rangle\rightarrow 0

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}((k-1)x_{i}-kx_{i+1})\rightarrow

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}((k-1)x_{i}-x_{i+1})\rightarrow\sum_{m=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{i+1}}\langle m\rangle\rightarrow 0,

together with the exact sequence

0\rightarrow\xi_{m-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)\rightarrow\xi_{m}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{n}}\langle m\rangle\rightarrow 0,

which gives

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}((k-1)x_{1}-x_{2})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{2})]=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{m=1}^{k-1}\big([\xi_{m}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-[\xi_{m-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)]\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}((k-1)x_{i}-kx_{i+1})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})]\big).

Combining everything,

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(k\epsilon_{1})]=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}((k-1)\epsilon_{1})]-[\operatorname{sgn}^{k-1}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}((k-1)x_{i}-kx_{i+1})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})]\big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{m=1}^{k-1}\big([\xi_{m}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-[\xi_{m-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}((k-1)\epsilon_{1})]-\bigg([\operatorname{sgn}^{k-1}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})]\bigg)+\sum_{m=1}^{k-1}[\xi_{m-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)]

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}((k-1)x_{i}-kx_{i+1})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})]\big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{m=1}^{k-1}\bigg([\xi_{m}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\big({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-mx_{i}+(m-1)x_{i+1})+{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(mx_{i}-(m+1)x_{i+1})\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}((k-1)\epsilon_{1})]-\sum_{m=1-k}^{2-k}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(mx_{i}-(m+1)x_{i+1})]

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=0}^{k-2}[\xi_{m-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)]-\sum_{m=1}^{k-1}[\xi_{m}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}],

so
C_{w_{k\epsilon_{1}}}=-[\xi_{k-1}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1.10
when
G
is of type C.

It suffices to check that, under the map
\widetilde{E}^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{\operatorname{sgn}}\rightarrow K^{G^{\vee}}\!(\widetilde{U})
sending
e_{w}
to
C_{w}
, for each element
w\in c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}
, the element
w\cdot e_{1}
is sent to
T_{w}
. The formulas for
w\cdot 1
in terms of
e_{v}
are given as (recall from Proposition
3.6
that
-\epsilon_{1}=s_{1}\cdots s_{n}\cdots s_{0}
), for example:

\displaystyle w_{k\epsilon_{1}}\cdot 1=s_{0}\big((1-k)\epsilon_{1}\big)\cdot 1=

\displaystyle\ 1+\Big\lfloor\frac{k+1}{2}\Big\rfloor C_{s_{0}}+\Big\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\Big\rfloor C_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=0}^{k-1}(k-m-1)\big(C_{s_{1}s_{0}(-m\epsilon_{1})}+\cdots C_{s_{0}\cdots s_{n}\cdots s_{0}(-m\epsilon_{1})}\big)

\displaystyle w_{k\epsilon_{1}}\cdot 1=(-k\epsilon_{1})\cdot 1=

\displaystyle\ 1+\Big\lfloor\frac{k+1}{2}\Big\rfloor C_{s_{0}}+\Big\lfloor\frac{k}{2}\Big\rfloor C_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=0}^{k-1}(k-m)\big(C_{s_{1}s_{0}(-m\epsilon_{1})}+\cdots C_{s_{0}\cdots s_{n}\cdots s_{0}(-m\epsilon_{1})}\big).

Thus it suffices to check that

w_{k\epsilon_{1}}\cdot 1-w_{(k-1)\epsilon_{1}}\cdot 1=\begin{cases}\displaystyle C_{s_{0}}+\sum_{m=0}^{k-1}\big(C_{s_{1}s_{0}(-m\epsilon_{1})}+\cdots C_{s_{0}\cdots s_{n}\cdots s_{0}(-m\epsilon_{1})}\big)&k\equiv 1\pmod{2}\\
\displaystyle C_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{0}}+\sum_{m=0}^{k-1}\big(C_{s_{1}s_{0}(-m\epsilon_{1})}+\cdots C_{s_{0}\cdots s_{n}\cdots s_{0}(-m\epsilon_{1})}\big)&k\equiv 0\pmod{2}\end{cases}

is sent to
t_{k\epsilon_{1}}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{(k-1)\epsilon_{1}}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]
. But this is exactly the last part of the proof of Proposition
5.10
.
∎

5.4.
When
G
is of type F
4

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
G
be of type F
4
, and let
\gamma=\sum_{i=1}^{4}a_{i}\epsilon_{i}\in Q^{\vee}
. Then

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\big((a_{2}-a_{4})x_{1}+(a_{4}-a_{3})y_{1}\big)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\big((a_{2}-a_{4})x_{1}+(a_{3}-a_{2})y_{2}\big)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(a_{4}x_{2})

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\big(\frac{1}{2}(a_{1}-a_{2}-a_{3}-a_{4})x_{3}\big).

Proof.

By Lemma
2.5
we know the underlying line bundle of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(\gamma)
is
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(a_{2}-a_{3})
and the underlying bundle of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(\gamma)
is
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(a_{3}-a_{4})
, so it suffices to calculate the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-equivariance of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}(\gamma)
at
x_{1}
. Observing that
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(m\alpha_{1})\simeq\omega_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}^{\otimes-m}
, we see the equivariant structure on
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}\!(m\alpha_{1})
at
x_{1}
is
(-1)^{m}
. Similarly,
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}\!(m\alpha_{2})
at
x_{1}
is
(-1)^{m}
. Moreover,
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}(\alpha_{3})
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}(\alpha_{4})
has trivial equivariant structure at
x_{1}
since
x_{1}
is not in
\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}
or
\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}
. Thus in general the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-equivariant structure of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}(\gamma)
at
x_{1}
is
(-1)^{a_{1}-a_{3}}
.
∎

We proceed as in §
5.2
. Repeatedly using Lemma
2.29
and Lemma
5.13
, we obtain:

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(-y_{1})]

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{4})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{4})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{4})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{4})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})]

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{3})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{4})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(-y_{2})]

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{3})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})].

Thus, we obtain the following description of the canonical basis of F
4
:

Theorem 5.14
.

Let
G
be of type F
4
. The canonical basis of
K^{G^{\vee}}\!(\widetilde{U})
are:

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=C_{w_{0}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]

\displaystyle C_{s_{0}}

\displaystyle=C_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}=-[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]

\displaystyle C_{s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=C_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(-y_{1})]

\displaystyle C_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=C_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{4}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle C_{s_{3}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=C_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{4}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]

\displaystyle C_{s_{4}s_{3}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=C_{w_{\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})]

\displaystyle C_{s_{2}s_{3}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=C_{w_{\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{3}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(-y_{2})]

\displaystyle C_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=C_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle C_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=C_{w_{2\epsilon_{1}}}=-[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}].

Finally, we may prove:

Proof of Proposition
1.10
when
G
if of type F
4
.

The proof is exactly the same as in type B.
∎

5.5.
When
G
is of type G
2

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be of type G
2
, and let
\gamma=a\alpha^{\vee}+b\beta^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}
. Then as
S_{3}
-equivariant line bundles:

•

{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(\gamma)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(3b-a,a)
.

•

{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(\gamma)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}((b-a)z+by)
.

We obtain the following description of the canonical basis of
K^{G^{\vee}}\!(\widetilde{U})
:

Proposition 5.16
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be of type G
2
. Then

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]

\displaystyle C_{s_{0}}

\displaystyle=-[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]

\displaystyle C_{s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]

\displaystyle C_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]

\displaystyle C_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,1)]

\displaystyle C_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]

\displaystyle C_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]

\displaystyle C_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]

\displaystyle C_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=-[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}].

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(2\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]

(51)

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(2\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)],

so
C_{s_{1}s_{0}}=C_{w_{\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]
.

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\alpha^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(\alpha^{\vee})]

(52)

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)],

so
C_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}=C_{w_{\alpha^{\vee}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]
. Note that in particular, since
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\alpha^{\vee})]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]
this implies

(53)

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)].

Again,

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-\alpha^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\alpha^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(-\alpha^{\vee})]

(54)

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\alpha^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,-1)]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\alpha^{\vee})]+[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)],

so
C_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}=C_{w_{-\alpha^{\vee}}}=[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]
, where the last equality is by twisting the Euler sequence (which is in particular
S_{3}
-equivariant)

(55)

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)\rightarrow\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,1)\rightarrow 0

by
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)
. Again,

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-\alpha^{\vee}-\beta^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-\alpha^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-\alpha^{\vee}-\beta^{\vee})]

(56)

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-\alpha^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)],

so
C_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}=C_{w_{-\alpha^{\vee}-\beta^{\vee}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]
.

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-2\alpha^{\vee}-\beta^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-\alpha^{\vee}-\beta^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(-2\alpha^{\vee}-\beta^{\vee})]

(57)

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(-\alpha-\beta)]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]

so
C_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}=C_{w_{-2\alpha^{\vee}-\beta^{\vee}}}=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]
. The computation for
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(3\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]
and
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(4\alpha^{\vee}+2\beta^{\vee})]
are slightly more complicated:

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(3\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(2\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(2\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(2\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(-1,0)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(2\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]

(58)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(2\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]

\displaystyle+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]+[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)],

where the third equality used the Euler sequence (
55
) and the last equality is (
53
) tensored by
\operatorname{std}
. Thus,
C_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}=C_{w_{3\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee}}}=-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]
. Similarly,

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(4\alpha^{\vee}+2\beta^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(3\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\beta}(3\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(3\alpha^{\vee}+2\beta^{\vee})]

(59)

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(3\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]-[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(3\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]-[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)],

where the last equality is (
53
) tensored by
\operatorname{sgn}
. Hence
C_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}=C_{w_{4\alpha^{\vee}+2\beta^{\vee}}}=-[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]
.
∎

Now, we have:

Proof of Proposition
1.10
when
G
is of type G
2
.

It suffices to check the basis in Proposition
5.10
satisfies (
7
).

(
51
) gives

s_{1}s_{0}C_{1}=C_{1}+C_{s_{0}}+C_{s_{1}s_{0}},

so

s_{1}C_{s_{0}}=s_{1}(-s_{0}C_{1}-C_{1})=-s_{1}s_{0}C_{1}+C_{1}=-C_{s_{0}}-C_{s_{1}s_{0}}.

Similarly,

s_{2}C_{s_{0}}=s_{2}(-s_{0}C_{1}-C_{1})=s_{0}C_{1}+C_{1}=-C_{s_{0}},

and

\displaystyle s_{0}C_{s_{0}}

\displaystyle=s_{0}(-s_{0}C_{1}-C_{1})

\displaystyle=-C_{1}-s_{0}C_{1}

\displaystyle=-C_{s_{0}}.

Together, we have (

) for

. Similarly, (

; (

\displaystyle=-s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}C_{1}+C_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

) for

6.
Re-phrase in the language of the Cartan of Kac-Moody algebras

In types B and F, we prove the following Conjecture of
[
BKK24
, §7]
, which describes Lusztig’s module
E_{\mathrm{subreg}}^{0}
in terms of the Cartan of Kac-Moody algebras. We recall the conjecture here. Let
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
be the affine Lie algebra associated to
\mathfrak{g}
, and let
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\vee}
be the dual Lie algebra.
7
7

7

Note that this is different from
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}}
, the affine Lie algebra associated to
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
.
Let
\mathfrak{k}
be the Lie algebra of an unfolding of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}^{\vee}
(so
\mathfrak{k}
is an un-cofolding of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
: long roots of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
correspond to multiple roots in
\mathfrak{k}
.)

Now there is an embedding
\widehat{W}\subset W(\mathfrak{k})
that sends a simple reflection of
\widehat{W}
to the product of simple reflections over the corresponding orbit in the unfolding. Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\subset\mathfrak{k}
be the integral form of the reflection representation of
W(\mathfrak{k})
, and let
\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}d
be the Cartan representation.

Formally, let
\{\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{m}\}
be the vertices of the Dynkin diagram of
\mathfrak{k}
, where
\alpha_{1}
is in the orbit of the affine vertex of the Dynkin diagram of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
. Then
W(\mathfrak{k})
is the group generated by
s_{1},\dots,s_{m}
with relations
s_{i}^{2}=1
, and
(s_{i}s_{j})^{2}=1
if
\alpha_{i}
and
\alpha_{j}
are not connected on the Dynkin diagram and
(s_{i}s_{j})^{3}=1
if
\alpha_{i}
and
\alpha_{j}
are connected.
8
8

8

By definition,
\mathfrak{k}
is simply-laced.
Then
\widetilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is the free abelian group generated by the
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{m}
such that

s_{i}\alpha_{j}=\begin{cases}-\alpha_{j}&i=j\\
\alpha_{j}&\text{$\alpha_{i}$ and $\alpha_{j}$ are not connected}\\
\alpha_{j}+\alpha_{i}&\text{$\alpha_{i}$ and $\alpha_{j}$ are connected}.\end{cases}

The Cartan representation
\widetilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}=\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}h
is a one-dimensional extension, defined by:

s_{i}h=\begin{cases}h&i\neq 1\\
h-\alpha_{1}&i=1.\end{cases}

Then:

Proposition 6.1

(
[
BKK24
, Conjecture 7.3]
)
.

Assume
\mathfrak{g}
is of type
\text{B}_{n}
with
n\geqslant 3
, or
F_{4}
. Then there are isomorphisms of
\widehat{W}
-modules

K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e})\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{\operatorname{sgn}},\hskip 8.5359ptK^{G^{\vee}}\!(\widetilde{U})\simeq\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{\operatorname{sgn}}.

Proof.

By Proposition
1.10
, it suffices to check the isomorphisms

E^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}},\hskip 8.5359pt\widetilde{E}^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}\simeq\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}.

When
\mathfrak{g}
is of type
\text{B}_{n}
, the Dynkin diagram of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
is

\alpha_{0}
\alpha_{1}
\alpha_{2}
\alpha_{3}
\alpha_{n-2}
\alpha_{n-1}
\alpha_{n}

so the Dynkin diagram of
\mathfrak{k}
looks like

\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}
\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}
\widetilde{\alpha}_{2}
\widetilde{\alpha}_{3}
\widetilde{\beta}_{3}
\widetilde{\beta}_{2}
\widetilde{\beta}_{0}
\widetilde{\beta}_{1}

where the middle vertex is
\widetilde{\alpha}_{n}=\widetilde{\beta}_{n}
. Let
s_{i}\in W(\mathfrak{k})
be the reflection corresponding to
\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}
and let
t_{i}\in W(\mathfrak{k})
be the reflection corresponding to
\widetilde{\beta}_{i}
. The associated embedding of the Coxeter groups is given as (see
[
Lus83
, Section 3.3]
)

\displaystyle\widehat{W}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow W(\mathfrak{k})

(63)

\displaystyle s_{\alpha_{i}}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow\begin{cases}s_{i}t_{i}&i<n\\
s_{n}&i=n.\end{cases}

Note that the Dynkin graph of
\mathfrak{k}
is isomorphic to the graph
\Gamma_{0}
in Proposition
3.6
, and there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}

\displaystyle\simeq\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}

\displaystyle e_{1}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow d

\displaystyle e_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow-\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}

\displaystyle e_{w_{\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}}}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow-\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}

\displaystyle e_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{i+1}}}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow-\widetilde{\alpha}_{i}\text{ for $2\leqslant i\leqslant n-1$}

\displaystyle e_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{i}}}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow-\widetilde{\beta}_{i}\text{ for $i\geqslant 2$}

\displaystyle e_{w_{\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1}}}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow-\widetilde{\beta}_{1}

\displaystyle e_{w_{2\epsilon_{2}}}

\displaystyle\mapstochar\rightarrow-\widetilde{\beta}_{0}

The proof for F
4
is similar, by observing that the Dynkin diagram of
\mathfrak{k}
is of type
\widetilde{\text{E}}_{7}
, the same as Proposition
3.8
.
∎

Let us now generalize Proposition
6.1
to the case of arbitrary (simple) Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
.
Let us identify
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e})
,
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}(\widetilde{U})
with Cartan subalgebras of certain Lie algebras and describe the
\widehat{W}
-module structures in these terms.

Let
\mathfrak{l}
be the Kac-Moody Lie algebra corresponding to the diagram
\Gamma_{0}
. Let
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{t}}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\subset\mathfrak{l}
be the integral form of the reflection representation of
W(\mathfrak{l})
. Recall that the vertices of
\Gamma_{0}
are parameterized by the elements of
c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}
. For
w\in c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}
we denote by
\alpha_{w}
the corresponding simple root of
\mathfrak{l}
, and by
e_{\alpha_{w}}
,
f_{\alpha_{w}}
the corresponding Chevalley generators. Consider the grading on
\mathfrak{l}
given by

\operatorname{deg}e_{\alpha_{s_{0}}}=1,\,\operatorname{deg}f_{\alpha_{s_{0}}}=-1,\,\operatorname{deg}e_{\alpha_{w}}=\operatorname{deg}f_{\alpha_{w}}=0\penalty\ \text{for}\penalty\ w\neq s_{0},\,\operatorname{deg}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{t}}}_{\mathbb{Z}}=0.

Let
\mathfrak{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathfrak{l}\oplus\mathbb{C}h
be the corresponding extension of
\mathfrak{k}
: the operator
\operatorname{ad}(h)
acts on
\mathfrak{k}
via the grading as above. Set
\widehat{{\mathfrak{t}}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\widetilde{{\mathfrak{t}}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}\oplus{\mathbb{Z}}h
. By abusing notation, for
w\in c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}
we denote by
\alpha_{w}\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{t}}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}
the
\widehat{{\mathfrak{t}}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
-weight of
e_{\alpha_{w}}
.

Remark 6.2
.

Recall that for
\mathfrak{g}=\text{B}_{n}
the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{k}
will be affine of type
\widetilde{\text{D}}_{2n}
, and for
\mathfrak{g}=\text{F}_{4}
the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{k}
will be affine of type
\widetilde{\text{E}}_{7}
. Note that for
\mathfrak{g}=\text{G}_{2}
,
\mathfrak{k}
will also be the affine Lie algebra, corresponding to
\widetilde{\text{E}}_{7}
. So, in all of these cases,
\mathfrak{k}
will be an affine Lie algebra (without any twisting).

Recall that
W(\mathfrak{l})=W(\Gamma_{0})
, the Coxeter group associated to the graph
\Gamma_{0}
, has generators
s_{\alpha_{w}}
for
w\in c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}
, with relations
s_{\alpha_{w}}^{2}=1
and
(s_{\alpha_{w}}s_{\alpha_{w^{\prime}}})^{2}=1
if
w
and
w^{\prime}
are disconnected in
\Gamma_{0}
and
(s_{\alpha_{w}}s_{\alpha_{w^{\prime}}})^{3}=1
if
w
and
w^{\prime}
are connected in
\Gamma_{0}
. Since the diagram
\Gamma_{0}
is possibly infinite, we consider a completion of
W(\mathfrak{l})
:

W(\mathfrak{l})^{\land}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
n\end{subarray}}W(\Gamma_{0}^{\leqslant n}),

where
\Gamma_{0}^{\leqslant n}\subset\Gamma
is the induced subgraph supported on

(c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}})^{\leqslant n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{w\in c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}:\ell(w)\leqslant n\}\subset c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}.

Since each vertex in
\Gamma_{0}
is only connected to finitely many other elements of
c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}
, the action of
W(\mathfrak{l})
on
\widetilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
extends to the completion
W(\mathfrak{l})^{\land}
.
Now, similar to (
63
), there is an embedding

\displaystyle\widehat{W}

\displaystyle\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow W(\mathfrak{l})^{\land}

\displaystyle s\in\widehat{S}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}\\
\mu(w)=s\end{subarray}}s_{\alpha_{w}}.

Note that even though the product is possibly infinite (in types A and C), the product is well-defined in the completion
W(\mathfrak{l})^{\land}
.

Using the embedding as above, we obtain an action of
\widehat{W}
on
\widetilde{{\mathfrak{t}}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
and
\widehat{{\mathfrak{t}}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

There are isomorphisms of
\widehat{W}
-modules

K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e})\simeq\widetilde{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{\operatorname{sgn}},\hskip 8.5359ptK^{G^{\vee}}\!(\widetilde{U})\simeq\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{\operatorname{sgn}}.

These isomorphisms send
[C_{w}]
to
-\alpha_{w}
for
w\in c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}
, and
[C_{1}]
maps to
h
.

Proof.

By direct computation.
∎

7.
Calculation of special values of Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials

7.1.
When
G
is of type B

We hope to express
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\gamma)\in K^{G^{\vee}}\!(\widetilde{U})
in terms of the canonical basis, where
\gamma=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+\cdots+a_{n-1}\alpha_{n-1}^{\vee}+b\beta^{\vee}
. By a repeated application of Lemma
2.29
, we know
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]
equals

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\gamma)]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{a_{1}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(i\alpha_{1}^{\vee})]+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{2}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+i\alpha_{2}^{\vee})]+\cdots+\sum_{i=0}^{b-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+\cdots+a_{n-1}\alpha_{n-1}^{\vee}+i\beta^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{a_{1}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(ix_{1}+ix_{2})]+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{2}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}((i-a_{1})x_{2}+ix_{3})]+\cdots+\sum_{i=0}^{b-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}((2i-a_{n-1})y)],

which is now a class in
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e})
. We calculate
(1+\operatorname{sgn})
and
(1-\operatorname{sgn})
times the above class.

Proposition 7.1
.

Adopt the convention that
a_{0}=0
. Then

(1+\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\big)=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}a_{j}(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}\!(-x_{j})]+2b[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]+\|\gamma\|[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}],

where
\|\gamma\|=a_{1}^{2}+\cdots+a_{n-1}^{2}+2b^{2}-(a_{1}a_{2}+\cdots+a_{n-1}a_{n-1}+2a_{n-1}b)
is the square length of
\gamma
normalized so the short root has length
1
.

Proof.

Note that
(1+\operatorname{sgn})(\operatorname{sgn}^{k}\otimes[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{i}}])=[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}]
in
K
-theory for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, so:

\displaystyle(1+\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\sum_{i=0}^{a_{j}-1}\big((1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}]+(2i-a_{j-1})[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}]\big)+2\sum_{i=0}^{b-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+(2i-a_{n-1})[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}]\big)

\displaystyle=(1+\operatorname{sgn})\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}a_{j}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}]+2b[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+\big(\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\sum_{i=0}^{a_{j}-1}(2i-a_{j-1})+2\sum_{i=0}^{b-1}(2i-a_{n-1})\big)[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}]

\displaystyle=(1+\operatorname{sgn})\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}a_{j}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}]+2b[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+N[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}]

where
N\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}(a_{j}^{2}-a_{j}-a_{j-1}a_{j})+2(b^{2}-b-a_{n-1}b)
. Finish by using:

\displaystyle(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}]-[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}]

\displaystyle=(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}\!(-x_{j})]

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)],

and
N=\|\gamma\|-a_{1}-\cdots-a_{n-1}-2b
.
∎

To calculate
(1-\operatorname{sgn})([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}])
, we first have:

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
k,\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and let
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
be arbitrary. Then in
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e})
,

(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(mx_{i}+nx_{i+1})]=(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}]+\operatorname{sgn}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{m})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{i}}]+\operatorname{sgn}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{n})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{i+1}}].

Proof.

By induction on the exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}((m-1)x_{i}+nx_{i+1})\rightarrow

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(mx_{i}+nx_{i+1})\rightarrow\operatorname{sgn}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{i}}\rightarrow 0

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(mx_{i}+(n-1)x_{i+1})\rightarrow

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(mx_{i}+nx_{i+1})\rightarrow\operatorname{sgn}^{n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{i+1}}\rightarrow 0.\qed

Proposition 7.3
.

In
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e})
,

(1-\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\big)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{j}-a_{j-1}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j}}].

Proof.

By Lemma
7.2
, and observing that
1-\operatorname{sgn}=\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn})^{2}
,

\displaystyle(1-\operatorname{sgn})\sum_{i=0}^{a_{j}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}((i-a_{j-1})x_{j}+ix_{j+1})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn})\sum_{i=0}^{a_{j}-1}\big((1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}]+\operatorname{sgn}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{i-a_{j-1}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j}}]

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\operatorname{sgn}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{i})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j+1}}]\big)

\displaystyle=a_{j}(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}]+\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn})\sum_{i=0}^{a_{j}-1}\big(\operatorname{sgn}^{i-a_{j-1}}[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j}}]+\operatorname{sgn}^{i}[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j+1}}]\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\frac{1}{2}a_{j}(1-\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j}}]+[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j+1}}]\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\operatorname{sgn}^{-a_{j-1}}-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{j}-a_{j-1}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j}}]+\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{j}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j+1}}],

where the last equation uses that
2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}]-[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j}}]-[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j}}]=(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}\!(-x_{j})]
is killed by
1-\operatorname{sgn}
. Thus, in total,

(1-\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\big)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{j}-a_{j-1}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j}}].\qed

Theorem 7.4
.

Let
G
be of type
\text{B}_{n}
where
n\geqslant 3
. For any
\gamma=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+\cdots+a_{n-1}\alpha_{n-1}^{\vee}+b\beta^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma\|+\frac{1}{4}\#\{1\leqslant j\leqslant n:a_{j-1}\not\equiv a_{j}\mod 2\}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{i+1}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\|\gamma\|-\frac{1}{2}a_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\#\{i<j\leqslant n:a_{j-1}\not\equiv a_{j}\mod 2\}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{n}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\|\gamma\|-b

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{i}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\|\gamma\|-\frac{1}{2}a_{i}-\frac{1}{2}\#\{i<j\leqslant n:a_{j-1}\not\equiv a_{j}\mod 2\}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{2\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma\|-\frac{1}{4}\#\{1\leqslant j\leqslant n:a_{j-1}\not\equiv a_{j}\mod 2\}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma\|-\Big\lceil\frac{a_{1}}{2}\Big\rceil+\frac{1}{4}\#\{1\leqslant j\leqslant n:a_{j-1}\not\equiv a_{j}\mod 2\}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{-\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma\|-\Big\lfloor\frac{a_{1}}{2}\Big\rfloor-\frac{1}{4}\#\{1\leqslant j\leqslant n:a_{j-1}\not\equiv a_{j}\mod 2\}

where we set
a_{0},a_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}0
.

Proof.

Combining Lemma
7.1
and Lemma
7.3
, with notation as in the lemmas, we obtain

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}a_{j}(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}\!(-x_{j})]+b[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]+\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma\|[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}]+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{j}-a_{j-1}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j}}].

Now, note that the formulae in Lemma
5.8
greatly simplify modulo
\ker(1-\operatorname{sgn})
:

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{1}}]

\displaystyle\equiv[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{j}}]

\displaystyle\equiv[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}d_{k}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{k}}}\!(-x_{k+1})]\text{ for }2\leqslant j\leqslant n.

Thus:

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}a_{j}(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}\!(-x_{j})]+b[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]+\frac{1}{4}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{j=2}^{n}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{j}-a_{j-1}})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}d_{k}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{k}}}\!(-x_{k+1})]\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma\|\big((1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}d_{j}(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}\!(-x_{j})]-2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}N(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{j}-a_{j-1}})[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\!(a_{j}-d_{j}\|\gamma\|)(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}\!(-x_{j})]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\sum_{1\leqslant k<j\leqslant n}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{j}-a_{j-1}})d_{k}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{k}}}\!(-x_{k+1})]+(b-\|\gamma\|)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)].

Since this equals
-\sum_{w_{\nu}\leqslant w_{\gamma},\nu\neq 1}\mathbf{m}_{w_{\nu}}^{w_{\gamma}}C_{\nu}
, we obtain the formulae above, where
d_{k}
was defined in (
36
).
∎

Remark 7.5
.

Although the expressions in the theorem are not obviously integers, they are. For example,
\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{i+1}}}^{w_{\gamma}}
are always integers since
\#\{i<j\leqslant n:a_{j-1}\not\equiv a_{j}\mod 2\}
is the number of sign changes in the sequence
a_{i},a_{i+1},\dots,a_{n-1},0
, which matches the parity of
a_{i}
. Moreover,
\|\gamma\|=a_{1}^{2}+\cdots+a_{n-1}^{2}+2b^{2}-(a_{1}a_{2}+\cdots+a_{n-2}a_{n-1}+2a_{n-1}b)
is always an integer.

For
w\in c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}
with
\mu(w)=i\in\widehat{S}
, we calculate
\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w}-\mathbf{m}_{w}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}
, which will be applied to computations of characters of representations of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
:

Corollary 7.6
.

Let
G
be of type
\text{B}_{n}
where
n\geqslant 3
. For
\gamma=a_{1}\alpha^{\vee}+\cdots+a_{n-1}\alpha_{n-1}^{\vee}+b\beta^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(a_{1}-1)-\frac{1}{2}(-1)^{a_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{i+1}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{i+1}}}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-a_{i}+\frac{1}{2}(a_{i-1}+a_{i+1})+\frac{1}{2}(-1)^{a_{i}-a_{i-1}}\mathbf{1}_{a_{i-1}\not\equiv a_{i+1}\bmod 2}&1<i<n-1\\
-a_{n-1}+\frac{1}{2}(a_{n-2}+b)+\frac{1}{2}(-1)^{a_{n-1}}\mathbf{1}_{a_{n-2}\not\equiv b\bmod 2}&i=n-1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{i}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{i}}}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-a_{i}+\frac{1}{2}(a_{i-1}+a_{i+1})-\frac{1}{2}(-1)^{a_{i}-a_{i-1}}\mathbf{1}_{a_{i-1}\not\equiv a_{i+1}\bmod 2}&i<n\\
-2b&i=n\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{2\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{2\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(a_{2}-1)+\frac{1}{2}(-1)^{a_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{s_{1}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lceil\frac{a_{2}-a_{1}}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lceil\frac{a_{1}}{2}\Big\rceil

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{-\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{-\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{s_{1}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{a_{2}-a_{1}}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lfloor\frac{a_{1}}{2}\Big\rfloor.

Proof.

For example, let us compute
\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{i+1}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{i+1}}}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}
for
1<i<n-1
; the rest of the computation is similar. First of all:

\displaystyle s_{i}\gamma

\displaystyle=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+\cdots+a_{i-2}\alpha_{i-2}^{\vee}+a_{i-1}(\alpha_{i-1}^{\vee}+\alpha_{i}^{\vee})-a_{i}\alpha_{i}^{\vee}+a_{i+1}(\alpha_{i}^{\vee}+\alpha_{i+1}^{\vee})+a_{i+2}\alpha_{i+2}^{\vee}+\cdots+b\beta^{\vee}

\displaystyle=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+\cdots+a_{i-1}\alpha_{i-1}^{\vee}+(a_{i-1}-a_{i}+a_{i+1})\alpha_{i}^{\vee}+a_{i+1}\alpha_{i+1}^{\vee}+\cdots+b\beta^{\vee}.

Note that since
|\gamma|=|s_{i}\gamma|
, we have:

\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{i+1}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{i+1}}}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}=-a_{i}+\frac{1}{2}(a_{i-1}+a_{i+1})+\frac{1}{2}(N_{1}-N_{2}),

where
N_{1}
is the number of parity changes in
a_{i},a_{i+1},\dots,a_{n},0
, while
N_{2}
is the number of parity changes in
a_{i-1}-a_{i}+a_{i+1},a_{i+1},\dots,a_{n},0
. Thus, simply:

\displaystyle N_{1}-N_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}_{a_{i}\not\equiv a_{i+1}\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{i-1}\not\equiv a_{i}\bmod 2}=(-1)^{a_{i}-a_{i+1}}\mathbf{1}_{a_{i-1}\not\equiv a_{i+1}\bmod 2}.\qed

7.2.
When
G
is of type C

Let
G
be of type
\text{C}_{n}
where
n\geqslant 2
.
As in type B, we observe

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{a_{1}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(i\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+a_{2}\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+\cdots+b\beta^{\vee})]+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{2}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(i\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+\cdots+b\beta^{\vee})]+\cdots+\sum_{i=0}^{b-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(i\beta^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{a_{1}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(ix_{1}+(i-a_{2})x_{2})]+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{2}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(ix_{2}+(i-a_{3})x_{3})]+\cdots+\sum_{i=0}^{b-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-i,i)].

For the computation, we note that

[\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{x_{j}}\langle k\rangle]=[\xi_{k}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-[\xi_{k-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)]+\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(kx_{i}-(k+1)x_{i+1})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}((k-1)x_{i}-kx_{i+1})]\big),

so

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}(Mx_{j}+Nx_{j+1})]=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}(-(N+1)x_{j}+Nx_{j+1})]+\sum_{k=-M}^{N}\big([\xi_{k}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-[\xi_{k-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)]\big)

(64)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(Mx_{i}-(M+1)x_{i+1})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-(N+1)x_{i}+Nx_{i+1})]\big).

Theorem 7.7
.

Let
G
be of type
\text{C}_{n}
where
n\geq 2
, and let
\gamma=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}a_{i}\alpha^{\vee}_{i}+b\beta^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}
. For
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lceil\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|\Big\rceil

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|\Big\rfloor

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{(k+1)\epsilon_{i+1}}}\Xi^{k}+\sum_{k<0}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{i}}}\Xi^{k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{i}\frac{\Xi^{a_{j}-a_{j-1}}-1}{\Xi-1}-\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\bigg(\frac{\Xi^{(a_{j}-a_{j+1})/2}-\Xi^{(a_{j+1}-a_{j})/2}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 2}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{1}}}(\Xi^{k-1}+\Xi^{1-k})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\bigg(\Big(\frac{\Xi^{(a_{j}-a_{j+1})/2}-\Xi^{(a_{j+1}-a_{j})/2}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}\Big)^{2}-|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|\bigg)

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{-k\epsilon_{n}}}\Xi^{k-1/2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{n}\frac{\Xi^{a_{j}-a_{j+1}-1/2}+\Xi^{a_{j+1}-a_{j}+1/2}-\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}{(\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2})^{2}},

where we let
a_{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}0
,
a_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}b
, and
a_{n+1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}0
.

Remark 7.8
.

Note that Theorem
7.7
gives a formula for
\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w}
for
w\in c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}
, since one can explicitly compute the coefficients of the polynomials on the right-hand side. Unlike in other types the
\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w}
are
not
quasi-polynomial functions of
\gamma
, as explained in the Appendix
B
.

Proof.

First, note that by Lemma
2.9
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{b-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-i,i)]

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{b-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(b-1-i,i)]\Big)\cdot[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1-b,0)]

\displaystyle=[\operatorname{sgn}^{b-1}\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{b-1}(\xi_{1/2})]\cdot\big([\mathrm{Sym}^{b-1}(\xi_{1/2})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-[\mathrm{Sym}^{b-2}(\xi_{1/2})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)]\big).

On the other hand, by (
64
), for each
j
, the sum
\sum_{m=0}^{a_{j}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}\!(mx_{j}+(m-a_{j+1})x_{j+1})]
equals

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{a_{1}-1}\bigg({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}(-(m-a_{j+1}+1)x_{j}+(m-a_{j+1})x_{j+1})

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}\big({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(mx_{i}-(m+1)x_{i+1})-{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-(m-a_{j+1}+1)x_{i}+(m-a_{j+1})x_{i+1})\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle+[V_{j}(\gamma)\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-[W_{j}(\gamma)\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)],

where
V_{j}(\gamma)
and
W_{j}(\gamma)
are representations of
\mathrm{Pin}_{2}
characterized by having characters with value
0
on
\mathrm{Pin}_{2}\backslash\mathrm{Spin}_{2}
and taking values

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{a_{j}-1}\frac{t^{m+1/2}-t^{-m-1/2}+t^{m-a_{j+1}+1/2}-t^{a_{j+1}-m-1/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}=

\displaystyle\ \frac{t^{1/2}+t^{1/2-a_{j+1}}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\sum_{m=0}^{a_{j}-1}t^{m}-\frac{t^{-1/2}+t^{a_{j+1}-1/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\sum_{m=0}^{a_{j}-1}t^{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{t^{1/2}+t^{1/2-a_{j+1}}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\cdot\frac{t^{a_{j}-1/2}-t^{-1/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{t^{-1/2}+t^{a_{j+1}-1/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\cdot\frac{t^{1/2}-t^{1/2-a_{j}}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{t^{a_{j}-a_{j+1}}+t^{a_{j+1}-a_{j}}+t^{a_{j}}+t^{-a_{j}}-t^{a_{j+1}}-t^{-a_{j+1}}-2}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{a_{j}-1}\frac{t^{m+1}-t^{-m-1}+t^{m-a_{j+1}}-t^{a_{j+1}-m}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}=

\displaystyle\ \frac{t+t^{-a_{j+1}}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\sum_{m=0}^{a_{j}-1}t^{m}-\frac{t^{-1}+t^{a_{j+1}}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\sum_{m=0}^{a_{j}-1}t^{-m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{t+t^{-a_{j+1}}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\cdot\frac{t^{a_{j}-1/2}-t^{-1/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}-\frac{t^{-1}+t^{a_{j+1}}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\cdot\frac{t^{1/2}-t^{1/2-a_{j}}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{t^{a_{j}-a_{j+1}-1/2}+t^{a_{j+1}-a_{j}+1/2}+t^{a_{j}+1/2}+t^{-a_{j}-1/2}}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{-t^{a_{j+1}+1/2}-t^{-a_{j+1}-1/2}-(t^{1/2}+t^{-1/2})}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}},

respectively, on
t^{1/2}\in\mathrm{Spin}_{2}
. In particular, their dimension is both
2a_{j}(a_{j}-a_{j+1})
. Thus, in total,
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\gamma)]
equals

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\sum_{m=0}^{a_{j}-1}\bigg([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{j}}}(-(m-a_{j+1}+1)x_{j}+(m-a_{j+1})x_{j+1})]

(65)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=j}^{n-1}\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(mx_{i}-(m+1)x_{i+1})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(-(m-a_{j+1}+1)x_{i}+(m-a_{j+1})x_{i+1})]\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle+[V(\gamma)\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]-[W(\gamma)\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)],

where
V(\gamma)=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}V_{j}(\gamma)+\mathrm{Sym}^{b-1}(\xi_{1/2})^{\otimes 2}
and
W(\gamma)=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}W_{j}(\gamma)+\mathrm{Sym}^{b-1}(\xi_{1/2})\otimes\mathrm{Sym}^{b-2}(\xi_{1/2})
. The characters of
V(\gamma)
and
W(\gamma)
at
(t^{1/2},1)\in\widetilde{\mathrm{SO}}_{2}
are (letting
a_{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}0
and
a_{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}b
):

\displaystyle\tr V(\gamma)(t)=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\frac{t^{a_{j}-a_{j+1}}+t^{a_{j+1}-a_{j}}}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}+\frac{t^{a_{1}}+t^{-a_{1}}-t^{b}-t^{-b}}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}-\frac{2(n-1)}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}+\big(\frac{t^{b/2}-t^{-b/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\big)^{2},

(66)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{t^{a_{j}-a_{j+1}}+t^{a_{j+1}-a_{j}}-2}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\bigg(\frac{t^{(a_{j}-a_{j+1})/2}-t^{(a_{j+1}-a_{j})/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\bigg)^{2},

and

\displaystyle\tr W(\gamma)(t)=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\frac{t^{a_{j}-a_{j+1}-1/2}+t^{a_{j+1}-a_{j}+1/2}}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}+\frac{t^{a_{1}+1/2}+t^{-a_{1}-1/2}-t^{b+1/2}-t^{-b-1/2}}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{(n-1)(t^{1/2}+t^{-1/2})}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}+\frac{t^{b/2}-t^{-b/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\cdot\frac{t^{(b-1)/2}-t^{(1-b)/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}

(67)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\frac{t^{a_{j}-a_{j+1}-1/2}+t^{a_{j+1}-a_{j}+1/2}}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}+\frac{t^{a_{1}+1/2}+t^{-a_{1}-1/2}-t^{b+1/2}-t^{-b-1/2}}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{(n-1)(t^{1/2}+t^{-1/2})}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}}+\frac{t^{b/2}-t^{-b/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}\cdot\frac{t^{(b-1)/2}-t^{(1-b)/2}}{t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n}\frac{t^{a_{j}-a_{j+1}-1/2}+t^{a_{j+1}-a_{j}+1/2}-t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2}}{(t^{1/2}-t^{-1/2})^{2}},

where
a_{n+1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}0
, and their character values on any
g=(t^{1/2},-1)\in{\mathrm{O}}_{2}\backslash{\mathrm{SO}}_{2}
are:

\displaystyle\tr V(\gamma)(g)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1&b\equiv 1\pmod{2}\\
0&b\equiv 1\pmod{2}.\end{cases}

\displaystyle\tr W(\gamma)(g)

\displaystyle=0.

Recalling that
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}\!(-x_{i+1})]
and from Proposition
5.12
that

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{i}}}(kx_{i}-(k+1)x_{i+1})]=\begin{cases}C_{w_{(k+1)\epsilon_{i+1}}}&k\geqslant 0\\
C_{w_{k\epsilon_{i}}}&k<0,\end{cases}

from (
65
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{(k+1)\epsilon_{i+1}}}\Xi^{k}+\sum_{k<0}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{i}}}\Xi^{k}=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{a_{i}-1}\Xi^{-(m-a_{i+1}+1)}+\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{m=0}^{a_{j}-1}\big(\Xi^{m}-\Xi^{-(m-a_{j+1}+1)}\big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\bigg(\frac{\Xi^{(a_{j}-a_{j+1})/2}-\Xi^{(a_{j+1}-a_{j})/2}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{i}\frac{\Xi^{a_{j}-a_{j-1}}-1}{\Xi-1}-\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\bigg(\frac{\Xi^{(a_{j}-a_{j+1})/2}-\Xi^{(a_{j+1}-a_{j})/2}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}\bigg)^{2}.

Next, since
\mathbf{m}_{w_{k\epsilon_{1}}}^{w_{\gamma}}=-[\xi_{k-1}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]
, equations (
66
) and (
67
) gives:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 2}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{1}}}(\Xi^{k-1}+\Xi^{1-k})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\bigg(\Big(\frac{\Xi^{(a_{j}-a_{j+1})/2}-\Xi^{(a_{j+1}-a_{j})/2}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}\Big)^{2}-|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k\geqslant 2}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{-k\epsilon_{n}}}\Xi^{k-1/2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{\Xi^{a_{j}-a_{j+1}-1/2}+\Xi^{a_{j+1}-a_{j}+1/2}-\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}{(\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2})^{2}}-\frac{\Xi^{\mathrm{b}}+\Xi^{-b}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}.

The computation for
\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbf{m}_{w_{-k\epsilon_{n}}}^{w_{\gamma}}(\Xi^{k-1/2}+\Xi^{1/2-k})
is the same, since
C_{w_{-k\epsilon_{n}}}=[\xi_{k-1/2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(1,0)]
.

Finally, note that
\dim V(\gamma)_{1}+\dim V(\gamma)_{\operatorname{sgn}}
, the sum of the
1
and
\operatorname{sgn}
-isotropic subspaces of
V(\gamma)
, is exactly the constant term of
\tr V(\gamma)(t)
above, which is simply
\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|
. Thus, the multiplicity of
1
and
\operatorname{sgn}
in
V(\gamma)
is
\lceil\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|\rceil
and
\lceil\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|\rceil
, respectively. Thus,

(68)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lceil\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|\Big\rceil

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{b\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}

(69)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|\Big\rfloor

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{b\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}.\qed

As a consequence, we may compute:

Corollary 7.9
.

Let
\gamma=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}a_{i}\alpha_{i}^{\vee}+b\beta^{\vee}
. For
1\leqslant i\leqslant n-1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(|a_{1}|-|a_{1}-1|-(-1)^{b})

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(|a_{1}|-|a_{1}-1|+(-1)^{b})

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 0}(\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{(k+1)\epsilon_{i+1}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}_{w_{(k+1)\epsilon_{i+1}}})\Xi^{k}+\sum_{k<0}(\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{i}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{i}}})\Xi^{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Xi^{a_{i}-a_{i-1}}-\Xi^{a_{i+1}-a_{i}}}{\Xi-1}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 2}(\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{1}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{1}}})(\Xi^{k-1}+\Xi^{1-k})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Xi^{a_{1}-1/2}-\Xi^{1/2-a_{1}}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}-(|a_{1}|-|a_{1}-1|)

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 1}(\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{-k\epsilon_{n}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{n}\gamma}}_{w_{-k\epsilon_{n}}})(\Xi^{k-1/2}+\Xi^{1/2-k})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Xi^{b-a_{n-1}}-\Xi^{a_{n-1}-b}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}\cdot\frac{\Xi^{a_{n-1}-1/2}-\Xi^{1/2-a_{n-1}}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}.

Proof.

Note that:

s_{0}\gamma=(1-a_{1})\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+(1+a_{2}-2a_{1})\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+(1+a_{3}-2a_{1})\alpha_{3}^{\vee}+\cdots+(1+b-2a_{1})\beta^{\vee}.

Thus, visibly,
|a_{j}-a_{j+1}|
is invariant under
s_{0}
for
j>0
. Thus, using (
68
) and (
69
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}|a_{1}|-\frac{1}{2}|1-a_{1}|+\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{1}_{b\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{1+b-2a_{1}\not\equiv 0}\bmod 2)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(|a_{1}|-|a_{1}-1|-(-1)^{b})

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(|a_{1}|-|a_{1}-1|+(-1)^{b}).

Similarly, most terms in the expression for
\sum_{k\geqslant 2}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{1}}}(\Xi^{k-1}+\Xi^{1-k})
is invariant under
s_{0}
, so

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 2}(\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{1}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{1}}})(\Xi^{k-1}+\Xi^{1-k})

\displaystyle=\bigg(\Big(\frac{\Xi^{a_{1}/2}-\Xi^{-a_{1}/2}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}\Big)^{2}-|a_{1}|\bigg)-\bigg(\Big(\frac{\Xi^{(1-a_{1})/2}-\Xi^{-(1-a_{1})/2}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}\Big)^{2}-|1-a_{1}|\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Xi^{a_{1}-1/2}-\Xi^{1/2-a_{1}}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}-\big(|a_{1}|-|a_{1}-1|\big).

Similarly, note that

s_{i}\gamma=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+\cdots+a_{i-1}\alpha_{i-1}^{\vee}+(a_{i-1}-a_{i}+a_{i+1})\alpha_{i}^{\vee}+a_{i+1}\alpha_{i+1}^{\vee}+\cdots+b\beta^{\vee},

so everything except for
(\Xi^{a_{i}-a_{i-1}}-1)/(\Xi-1)
in the expression for

\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\mathbf{m}_{w_{(k+1)\epsilon_{i+1}})}^{w_{\gamma}}\Xi^{k}+\sum_{k<0}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{k\epsilon_{i}}\Xi^{k}
in Theorem
7.7

is invariant under
s_{i}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 0}(\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{(k+1)\epsilon_{i+1}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}_{w_{(k+1)\epsilon_{i+1}}})\Xi^{k}+\sum_{k<0}(\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{i}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}_{w_{k\epsilon_{i}}})\Xi^{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Xi^{a_{i}-a_{i-1}}-1}{\Xi-1}-\frac{\Xi^{a_{i+1}-a_{i}}-1}{\Xi-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Xi^{a_{i}-a_{i-1}}-\Xi^{a_{i+1}-a_{i}}}{\Xi-1}.

Finally,

s_{n}\gamma=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+\cdots+a_{n-1}\alpha_{n-1}^{\vee}+(2a_{n-1}-b)\beta^{\vee},

so again all terms in the expression for
\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{-k\epsilon_{n}}}\Xi^{k-1/2}
in Theorem
7.7
except for

\frac{\Xi^{b-1/2}+\Xi^{1/2-b}-\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}{(\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2})^{2}}
are
s_{n}
-invariant, and hence

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 1}(\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{-k\epsilon_{n}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{n}\gamma}}_{w_{-k\epsilon_{n}}})(\Xi^{k-1/2}+\Xi^{1/2-k})=

\displaystyle\ \frac{\Xi^{b-1/2}+\Xi^{1/2-b}-\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}{(\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2})^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{\Xi^{2a_{n-1}-b-1/2}+\Xi^{1/2-2a_{n-1}+b}-\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}{(\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2})^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{\Xi^{b-a_{n-1}}-\Xi^{a_{n-1}-b}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}\cdot\frac{\Xi^{a_{n-1}-1/2}-\Xi^{1/2-a_{n-1}}}{\Xi^{1/2}-\Xi^{-1/2}}.\qed

7.3.
When
G
is of type F
4

There are the identities:

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{4})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(-y_{2})]

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{4})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{4})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{3})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{4})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})],

which allows us to express
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}]
in terms of the canonical basis:

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(-y_{1})]-\big(2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(-y_{1})]\big)-2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})].

Thus:

Lemma 7.10
.

For
G
of type F
4
, in
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e})
,

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{C}}_{y_{1}}]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(-y_{1})]-\big(2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(-y_{2})]\big)-2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})]

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{1}}]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(-y_{1})]\big)-\big(2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(-y_{2})]\big)-2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})]

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{C}}_{y_{2}}]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(-y_{1})]\big)-\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]+2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(-y_{2})]\big)-2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})]

We can express
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\in K^{G^{\vee}}\!(\widetilde{U})
as follows, where
\gamma=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+a_{2}\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+a_{3}\alpha_{3}^{\vee}+a_{4}\alpha_{4}^{\vee}
:

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\gamma)]=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{a_{1}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(i\alpha_{1}^{\vee})]+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{2}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+i\alpha_{2}^{\vee})]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{3}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+a_{2}\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+i\alpha_{3}^{\vee})]+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{4}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+a_{2}\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+a_{3}\alpha_{3}^{\vee}+i\alpha_{4}^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{a_{1}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(ix_{1}+iy_{1})]+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{2}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}((a_{1}+i)x_{1}+(i-2a_{1})y_{2})]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{3}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}((2i-a_{2})x_{2})]+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{4}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}((2i-a_{3})x_{3})].

We calculate both
(1-\operatorname{sgn})([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}])
and
(1+\operatorname{sgn})([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}])
:

Lemma 7.11
.

For
G
of type F
4
and
\gamma=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+a_{2}\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+a_{3}\alpha_{3}^{\vee}+\alpha_{4}^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}

\displaystyle(1+\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\big)=

\displaystyle a_{1}(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]+a_{2}(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle+2a_{3}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]+2a_{4}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})]+\|\gamma\|[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{2}}]

in
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e})
, where
\|\gamma\|=a_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}+2a_{3}^{2}+2a_{4}^{2}-a_{1}a_{2}-2a_{2}a_{3}-2a_{3}a_{4}
is the square-length of
\gamma
normalized so the short roots have length
1
.

Proof.

Indeed

\displaystyle(1+\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\big)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{a_{1}-1}(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{2}-1}(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{2})]

\displaystyle+2\sum_{i=0}^{a_{3}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]+2\sum_{i=0}^{a_{4}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+\operatorname{sgn})a_{1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]+(1+\operatorname{sgn})a_{2}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle+2a_{3}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]+2a_{4}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})]+N[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{2}}],

where

\displaystyle N

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{a_{1}-1}(2i+1)+\sum_{i=0}^{a_{2}-1}(2i+1-a_{1})+2\sum_{i=0}^{a_{3}-1}(2i+1-a_{2})+2\sum_{i=0}^{a_{4}-1}(2i+1-a_{3})

\displaystyle=a_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}+2a_{3}^{2}+2a_{4}^{2}-a_{1}a_{2}-2a_{2}a_{3}-2a_{3}a_{4}

\displaystyle=\|\gamma\|.\qed

Moreover, by Lemma
7.10
,

[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{2}}]=(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-2(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]-3(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]-4[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]-2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})],

so

\displaystyle(1+\operatorname{sgn})([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}])=

\displaystyle\|\gamma\|(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+(a_{1}-2\|\gamma\|)(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle+(a_{2}-3\|\gamma\|)(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]+(2a_{3}-4\|\gamma\|)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]

\displaystyle+(2a_{4}-2\|\gamma\|)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})].

Lemma 7.12
.

For
G
of type F
4
and
\gamma=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+a_{2}\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+a_{3}\alpha_{3}^{\vee}+\alpha_{4}^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}
,

(70)

(1-\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\big)=\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{y_{1}}]+\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}+a_{2}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{1}}]+\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{2}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{y_{2}}].

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
7.3
, we have

\displaystyle(1-\operatorname{sgn})\sum_{i=0}^{a_{1}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(ix_{1}+iy_{1})]=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{1}}]+\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{y_{1}}]

\displaystyle(1-\operatorname{sgn})\sum_{i=0}^{a_{2}-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}((a_{1}+i)x_{1}+(i-2a_{1})y_{2})]=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}(\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}}-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}+a_{2}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{1}}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}(\operatorname{sgn}^{-2a_{1}}-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{2}-2a_{1}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{y_{1}}].\qed

However, we need to rewrite (
70
) in terms of the canonical basis. Now, by Lemma
7.10
, modulo
\ker(1-\operatorname{sgn})
, the following hold:

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{C}}_{y_{1}}]

\displaystyle\equiv[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(-y_{1})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{1}}]

\displaystyle\equiv[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{C}}_{y_{2}}]

\displaystyle\equiv[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(-y_{2})].

We therefore obtain:

Theorem 7.13
.

Let
G
be of type F
4
. Then for
\gamma=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+a_{2}\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+a_{3}\alpha_{3}^{\vee}+a_{4}\alpha_{4}^{\vee}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma\|+\frac{1}{4}\big(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}\big)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\|\gamma\|-\lfloor\frac{a_{1}}{2}\rfloor

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{4}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2}\|\gamma\|-\frac{1}{2}a_{2}+\frac{1}{4}\big(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\mod 2}\big)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{4}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=2\|\gamma\|-a_{3}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\|\gamma\|-a_{4}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2}\|\gamma\|-\frac{1}{2}a_{2}-\frac{1}{4}\big(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}\mod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}\big)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\|\gamma\|-\lceil\frac{a_{1}}{2}\rceil

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{2\epsilon_{1}}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma\|-\frac{1}{4}\big(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}\big).

Proof.

(
70
) can be re-written as:

\displaystyle(1-\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\big)=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{y_{1}}]+\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}+a_{2}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{x_{1}}]+\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{2}})[{\mathbb{C}}_{y_{2}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(-y_{1})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}+a_{2}})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]\big)+\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{2}})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(-y_{2})]\big).

Thus, since

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}(1+\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\big)+\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-t_{\gamma}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}\|\gamma\|(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+\big(\frac{1}{2}a_{1}-\|\gamma\|\big)(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle+(\frac{1}{2}a_{2}-\frac{3}{2}\|\gamma\|)(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]

\displaystyle+(a_{3}-2\|\gamma\|)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{3}}}\!(-x_{2})]+(a_{4}-\|\gamma\|)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{4}}}\!(-x_{3})]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-2[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{1}}}(-y_{1})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{1}+a_{2}})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}\!(-x_{1})]\big)+\frac{1}{4}(1-\operatorname{sgn}^{a_{2}})\big([{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha_{2}}}(-y_{2})]\big),

we obtain the desired formulae.
∎

Let us compute
\mathbf{m}_{w}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}
for
w\in\widehat{W}
and
\mu(w)=i\in\widehat{S}
:

Corollary 7.14
.

Let
G
be of type F
4
. Then for
\gamma=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+a_{2}\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+a_{3}\alpha_{3}^{\vee}+a_{4}\alpha_{4}^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big(a_{1}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\equiv 0\bmod 2}\big)
