\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{s_{1}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{a_{2}-a_{1}}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lfloor\frac{a_{1}}{2}\Big\rfloor

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{4}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{4}}}^{w_{s_{2}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=a_{3}-\frac{1}{2}a_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\mod 2}\big)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{4}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{4}}}^{w_{s_{3}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=a_{2}-2a_{3}+a_{4}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{s_{4}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=a_{3}-2a_{4}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{3}}}^{w_{s_{2}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=a_{3}-\frac{1}{2}a_{1}-\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\mod 2}\big)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{s_{1}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lceil\frac{a_{2}-a_{1}}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lceil\frac{a_{1}}{2}\Big\rceil

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{2\epsilon_{1}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{2\epsilon_{1}}}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\big(a_{1}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\equiv 0\bmod 2}\big)

Proof.

For
i\neq 0
, note that
\|s_{i}\gamma\|=\|\gamma\|
, so the computation greatly simplifies. For example, for
w=w_{\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}}
, since

s_{2}(\gamma)=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+(a_{1}-a_{2}+2a_{3})\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+a_{3}\alpha_{3}^{\vee}+a_{4}\alpha_{4}^{\vee},

we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{4}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{4}}}^{w_{s_{2}\gamma}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}a_{2}+\frac{1}{4}\big(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\mod 2}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}(a_{1}-a_{2}+2a_{3})-\frac{1}{4}\big(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{1}-a_{2}\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}-a_{2}\not\equiv 0\mod 2}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{3}-\frac{1}{2}a_{1}+\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\mod 2}).

When
i=0
, note that

s_{1}\gamma=(a_{1}+2)\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+(a_{2}+3)\alpha_{3}^{\vee}+(a_{3}+2)\alpha_{3}^{\vee}+(a_{3}-a_{4}+1)\alpha_{4}^{\vee}.

we have:

\langle\gamma,\gamma\rangle-\langle s_{1}\gamma,s_{1}\gamma\rangle=\langle\gamma,\gamma\rangle-\langle\gamma-\theta^{\vee},\gamma-\theta^{\vee}\rangle=2\langle\theta^{\vee},\gamma\rangle-\langle\theta^{\vee},\theta^{\vee}\rangle=a_{1}-1.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}(2a_{1}-1)+\frac{1}{4}\big(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\big(\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}+1\bmod 2}+\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}+1\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}\big(a_{1}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{1}\equiv a_{2}\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\equiv 0\bmod 2}\big).\qed

7.4.
When
G
is of type G
2

To calculate the special values of Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials, we prove some lemmas:

Lemma 7.15
.

For each
m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
there is a
{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}
-equivariant exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}\!(m,n-2)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}\!(m,n)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{n-m}\rightarrow 0,

where in
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e})
,

(71)

[\mathcal{F}_{k}]=\begin{cases}[(1+\operatorname{sgn})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]&\text{if }k\equiv 0\pmod{3}\\
[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-[(1+\operatorname{sgn})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]&\text{if }k\equiv 1\pmod{3}\\
[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]&\text{if }k\equiv 2\pmod{3}\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
{\mathrm{O}}_{2}=\mathbb{G}_{m}\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}/2\subset{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}
act on
\mathbf{P}^{1}
so
a\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts by
[x:y]\mapstochar\rightarrow[ax:a^{-1}y]
. Then by Corollary
2.11
we know
H^{0}(\mathbf{P}^{1},{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,2))\simeq{\mathbb{C}}+\xi_{2}
, so there is a non-zero homomorphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,2)
. Thus for any
m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
there is an exact sequence of
{\mathrm{O}}_{2}
-equivariant sheaves

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(m,n-2)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(m,n)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\rightarrow 0

where
\mathcal{F}
has length
2
. When
m<n
the cohomology long exact sequence gives

0\rightarrow H^{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(m,n-2))\rightarrow H^{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(m,n))\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\rightarrow 0,

so using Corollary
2.11
, we conclude
H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\simeq\xi_{n-m}
. When
m>n
the cohomology long exact sequence gives

0\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\rightarrow H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(m,n-2))\rightarrow H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(m,n))\rightarrow 0,

which by Corollary
2.11
combined with Serre duality again gives
H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\simeq\xi_{n-m}
. Finally, when
m=n
the cohomology longe exact sequence gives

0\rightarrow H^{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(m,m))=\operatorname{sgn}^{\otimes m}\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\rightarrow H^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(m,m-2))=\operatorname{sgn}^{m-1}\rightarrow 0,

which again gives
H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}+\operatorname{sgn}=\xi_{0}
. Since
\mathcal{F}
is
{\mathrm{O}}_{2}
-equivariant and of length
2
, it must be supported on
\{0,\infty\}\subset\mathbf{P}^{1}
. Moreover,
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on the stalk of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(m,n)
at
0
as
{\mathbb{C}}\langle n-m\rangle
, so
\mathcal{F}\simeq\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{0}\langle n-m\rangle\oplus\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{\infty}\langle m-n\rangle
. For convenience, let
\mathcal{F}_{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{0}\langle k\rangle\oplus\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{\infty}\langle-k\rangle
for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, considered as a
S_{3}\subset{\mathrm{O}}_{2}
-equivariant sheaf. From the description, it is clear that
\mathcal{F}_{k}
only depends on
k
modulo
3
, so it suffices to calculate
\mathcal{F}_{0}
,
\mathcal{F}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
.

Note that there is an exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(2,0)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{0}\rightarrow 0,

and the Euler sequence gives

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(2,0)\rightarrow\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(1,0)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(1,1)=\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}\rightarrow 0,

so in
K^{S_{3}}(\mathbf{P}^{1})
,

[\mathcal{F}_{0}]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(2,0)]=[(1+\operatorname{sgn})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(1,0)].

Similarly, the exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,-1)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,1)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{1}\rightarrow 0,

together with the Euler sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(1,0)\rightarrow\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,1)\rightarrow 0

gives

[\mathcal{F}_{1}]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,1)]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,-1)]=[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}]-[(1+\operatorname{sgn})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(1,0)].

Finally, there are exact sequences

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,2)\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{2}\rightarrow 0

and twists of the Euler sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,1)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,2)\rightarrow 0

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(1,0)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\operatorname{std}^{\otimes 2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}\rightarrow\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,1)\rightarrow 0,

which together gives

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}_{2}]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,2)]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}]

\displaystyle=[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(0,1)]-[(1+\operatorname{sgn})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}]

\displaystyle=[\operatorname{std}^{\otimes 2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}]-[(1+\operatorname{sgn})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(1,0)]

\displaystyle=[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(1,0)].\qed

Remark 7.16
.

Note that (
71
) is useful, since
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]
and tensor products of it with representations of
S_{3}
are already part of the canonical basis, and
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]
is expressed in terms of the canonical basis in (
53
).

Proposition 7.17
.

Let
G
be of type G
2
. Then for
\gamma=m\alpha^{\vee}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{1}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+N_{0}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+N_{0}+N_{1}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+\frac{m(m-1)}{2}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{m(m-1)}{2}+N_{2}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=N_{1}+N_{2}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor+\frac{m(m-1)}{2}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor+N_{0}+N_{1}

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor+N_{0},

where for
r\in\{0,1,2\}
,

(72)

N_{r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant j\leqslant m-1\\
j\equiv-r\bmod 3\end{subarray}}\!(m-j).

Proof.

By using Lemma
2.29
inductively, we obtain:

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(m\alpha^{\vee})]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(-i,i)].

Moreover by Lemma
7.15
, we have:

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(-i,i)]=[\operatorname{sgn}^{i}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]+\sum_{j=1}^{i}[\mathcal{F}_{2j}],

so in total

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(m\alpha^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}[\operatorname{sgn}^{i}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i}[\mathcal{F}_{2j}]

(73)

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}(m-j)[\mathcal{F}_{2j}]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-\lceil\frac{m}{2}\rceil[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-N_{0}[\mathcal{F}_{0}]-N_{1}[\mathcal{F}_{1}]-N_{2}[\mathcal{F}_{2}],

where
N_{r}
is as defined in (
72
).
Now, substituting the formulae in Lemma
7.15
for
[\mathcal{F}_{k}]
into (
73
) gives

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(m\alpha^{\vee})]=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-N_{0}\big([(1+\operatorname{sgn})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]\big)

\displaystyle-N_{1}\big([\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-[(1+\operatorname{sgn})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]\big)-N_{2}\big([\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,1)]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-\Big(\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+N_{0}\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor+N_{0}\Big)[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]-(N_{1}+N_{2})[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]

\displaystyle+N_{1}[(1+\operatorname{sgn})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]+(N_{0}+N_{2})[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)].

Finally, substituting (
53
) for
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]
gives

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(m\alpha^{\vee})]=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-\Big(\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+N_{0}\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor+N_{0}\Big)[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-(N_{1}+N_{2})[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]

\displaystyle+\Big(\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+\frac{m(m-1)}{2}\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]+\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor+\frac{m(m-1)}{2}\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]

\displaystyle+\Big(\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+N_{0}+N_{1}\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]+\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor+N_{0}+N_{1}\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]

\displaystyle+\Big(\frac{m(m-1)}{2}+N_{2}\Big)[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)],

where we use the identity
N_{0}+N_{1}+N_{2}=m(m-1)/2
repeatedly. We conclude by Proposition
5.16
.
∎

Remark 7.18
.

The
N_{r}(m)
are quasi-polynomials of
m
: for
r\in\{0,1,2\}
,

\displaystyle N_{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{\lfloor(m+r)/3\rfloor}\!(m+r-3j^{\prime})=\Big\lfloor\frac{m+r}{3}\Big\rfloor(m+r)-\frac{3}{2}\Big\lfloor\frac{m+r}{3}\Big\rfloor\cdot\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{m+r}{3}\Big\rfloor+1\Big).

Next, we finally compute the special values of Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomial for arbitrary
\gamma=m\alpha^{\vee}+n\beta^{\vee}
in type
G_{2}
:

Theorem 7.19
.

Let
G
be of type G
2
. Then for
\gamma=m\alpha^{\vee}+n\beta^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{1}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=N_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(n(n-m)+\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor+\Big\lceil\frac{n}{2}\Big\rceil\Big)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+N_{0}+N_{1}+n(n-m)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{m(m-1)}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(3n(n-m)+\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lceil\frac{n}{2}\Big\rceil\Big)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{m(m-1)}{2}+N_{2}+2n(n-m)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=N_{1}+N_{2}+n(n-m)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{m(m-1)}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(3n(n-m)+\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lceil\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor+N_{0}+N_{1}+n(n-m)

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=N_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(n(n-m)+\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lceil\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rceil+\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big),

where for
r\in\{0,1,2\}
,

N_{r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant j\leqslant m-1\\
j\equiv-r\bmod 3\end{subarray}}\!(m-j).

Proof.

By a repeated application of Lemma
2.29
, combined with Lemma
5.15
, we have

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(m\alpha^{\vee}+n\beta^{\vee})]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(m\alpha^{\vee})]-\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}((i-m)z+iy)].

Since the first term is calculated in Proposition
7.17
, it suffices to calculate the second term. By the standard exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}((a-1)z+by)\rightarrow

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(az+by)\rightarrow\operatorname{sgn}^{a}\otimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{z}\rightarrow 0

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(az+(b-1)y)\rightarrow

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(az+by)\rightarrow\operatorname{sgn}^{b}\otimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{y}\rightarrow 0,

we have

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(az+by)]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+\sum_{i=1}^{a}[\operatorname{sgn}^{i}\otimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{x}]+\sum_{i=1}^{b}[\operatorname{sgn}^{i}\otimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{y}].

Thus,

(74)

\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}((i-m)z+iy)]=n[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i-m}[\operatorname{sgn}^{j}\otimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{x}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i}[\operatorname{sgn}^{j}\otimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{y}].

Multiplying (
74
) by
1+\operatorname{sgn}
gives

\displaystyle(1+\operatorname{sgn})n[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+(1+\operatorname{sgn})\bigg(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i-m}[\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{x}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i}[\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{y}]\bigg)=

\displaystyle\ n(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+(1+\operatorname{sgn})n(n-m-1)[\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{x}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ n(n-m)(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]

\displaystyle-n(n-m-1)(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)].

On the other hand, multiplying (
74
) by
1-\operatorname{sgn}
gives

(75)

(1-\operatorname{sgn})n[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(\operatorname{sgn}-\operatorname{sgn}^{i-m+1})[\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{x}]+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(\operatorname{sgn}-\operatorname{sgn}^{i+1})[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}].

Observing that in the first sum only
i
with
i\not\equiv m\pmod{2}
contributes, and in the second sum only odd
i
contributes, we see (
75
) equals

(1-\operatorname{sgn})n[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+\Big(\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big)\big([\operatorname{sgn}\otimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{x}]-[{\mathbb{C}}_{x}]\big)+\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor\big([\operatorname{sgn}\otimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{y}]-[\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{y}]\big).

Further observing that:

\displaystyle[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{x}]-[{\mathbb{C}}_{x}]

\displaystyle=(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]-(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]

\displaystyle[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}_{y}]-[{\mathbb{C}}_{y}]

\displaystyle=-(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]-(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]

shows the above equals

\Big(n-\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big)(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+\Big(\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big)(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)].

Thus (
74
), which is
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(m\alpha^{\vee})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(m\alpha^{\vee}+n\beta^{\vee})]
, equals:

\displaystyle n(n-m)\frac{1+\operatorname{sgn}}{2}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]+\Big(\Big\lceil\frac{n}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big)\frac{1-\operatorname{sgn}}{2}[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]

(76)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\Big(n(n-m-1)-\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor+\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\Big(n(n-m-1)+\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)].

Thus, to finish we have to compute
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\beta^{\vee})]
in terms of the canonical basis: using Lemma
2.29
and (
51
) and Lemma
5.15
,

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\beta^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(\alpha^{\vee}+\beta^{\vee})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(\beta^{\vee})]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(3,0)]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]

\displaystyle=[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(2,0)],

where the last equality is by Euler’s sequence. Now again by Euler’s sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(2,0)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)\rightarrow\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}\rightarrow 0,

we have

\displaystyle[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(2,0)]=

\displaystyle\ [\operatorname{std}^{\otimes 2}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]+[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]

\displaystyle-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]+[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]+2[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]+[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)]-[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}],

where the last step uses (
53
). So in total,

[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]=[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]-2[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)].

Note that in particular:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}(1-\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}]=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}(1+\operatorname{sgn})[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]

\displaystyle-2[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)].

Thus (
76
), which is
[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(m\alpha^{\vee})]-[{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{U}}(m\alpha^{\vee}+n\beta^{\vee})]
, equals

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big(n(n-m)-\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor+\Big\lceil\frac{n}{2}\Big\rceil\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]+\frac{1}{2}\Big(n(n-m)+\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil+\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lceil\frac{n}{2}\Big\rceil\Big)[\operatorname{sgn}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]

\displaystyle+n(m-m)[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e}}]-n(n-m)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]-n(n-m)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(0,-1)]-2n(n-m)[\operatorname{std}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\alpha}}(1,0)]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\Big(3n(n-m)-\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lceil\frac{n}{2}\Big\rceil\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}\!(-x)]-\frac{1}{2}\Big(3n(n-m)-\Big\lfloor\frac{m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lceil\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big)[{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\beta}}(-y)].

Combining with Proposition
7.17
gives the desired special values of Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials.
∎

As usual, we compute
\mathbf{m}_{w}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{w}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}
for
w\in\widehat{W}
and
\mu(w)=i\in\widehat{S}
:

Corollary 7.20
.

Let
G
be of type G
2
. Then for
\gamma=m\alpha^{\vee}+n\beta^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{0}}-\mathbf{m}_{s_{0}}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=2\Big\lfloor\frac{m-1}{3}\Big\rfloor+\frac{1}{2}(m-\mathbf{1}_{m\equiv n\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{n\equiv 0\bmod 2})

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}_{s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{\gamma}}-\mathbf{m}_{s_{1}s_{0}}^{w_{s_{1}\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(n+\mathbf{1}_{m\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{m\not\equiv n\bmod 2})-\Big\lfloor\frac{m-2}{3}\Big\rfloor

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{2}\gamma}}_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lceil\frac{n}{2}\Big\rceil

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{1}\gamma}}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=n-m+\Big\lfloor\frac{m-2}{3}\Big\rfloor

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=-2\Big\lfloor\frac{m-1}{3}\Big\rfloor

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{2}\gamma}}_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=-\Big\lceil\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{1}\gamma}}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(n+\mathbf{1}_{m\equiv 0\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{m\equiv n\bmod 2})-\Big\lfloor\frac{m-2}{3}\Big\rfloor

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}

\displaystyle=2\Big\lfloor\frac{m-1}{3}\Big\rfloor+\frac{1}{2}(m-\mathbf{1}_{m\not\equiv n\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{n\not\equiv 0\bmod 2})

Proof.

Note: (see Remark
2.15
)

\displaystyle s_{0}\gamma

\displaystyle=(2-m)\alpha^{\vee}+(n-m+1)\beta^{\vee}

\displaystyle s_{1}\gamma

\displaystyle=(3n-m)\alpha^{\vee}+n\beta^{\vee}

\displaystyle s_{2}\gamma

\displaystyle=m\alpha^{\vee}+(m-n)\beta^{\vee}.

We will first calculate how the
N_{r}
change under the transformations, for
r\in\{0,1,2\}
:

\displaystyle N_{0}(m)-N_{0}(2-m)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant j\leqslant m-1\\
j\equiv 0\bmod 3\end{subarray}}\!(m-j)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant j\leqslant 1-m\\
j\equiv 0\bmod 3\end{subarray}}(2-m-j)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant j\leqslant m-1\\
j\equiv 0\bmod 3\end{subarray}}\big((m-j)+(2-m+j)\big)

\displaystyle=2\Big\lfloor\frac{m-1}{3}\Big\rfloor.

Similarly,

\displaystyle N_{2}(m)-N_{2}(3n-m)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant j\leqslant m-1\\
j\equiv 1\bmod 3\end{subarray}}\!(m-j)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant j\leqslant 3n-m-1\\
j\equiv 1\bmod 3\end{subarray}}(3n-m-j)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant j\leqslant m-1\\
j\equiv 1\bmod 3\end{subarray}}\!(m-j)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\leqslant j\leqslant 3n-2\\
j\equiv 1\bmod 3\end{subarray}}(j-m+1)

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{m-2}{3}\Big\rfloor-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant j\leqslant 3n-2\\
j\equiv 1\bmod 3\end{subarray}}(j-m+1)

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{m-2}{3}\Big\rfloor-\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}(3j^{\prime}-m-1)

\displaystyle=\Big\lfloor\frac{m-2}{3}\Big\rfloor-\frac{1}{2}n(3n-2m+1),

where for the second-to-last equality we made the change of variables
j=3j^{\prime}-2
. To compute
\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{0}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{s_{0}}
, it is first convenient to write:

\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{0}}=N_{0}(m)+\frac{1}{2}n(n-m+1)+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big),

since
n(n-m+1)
is invariant under
s_{0}
. Thus

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{0}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{s_{0}}=

\displaystyle\ N_{0}(m)-N_{0}(2-m)+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\Big\lceil\frac{m}{2}\Big\rceil-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lceil\frac{2-m}{2}\Big\rceil+\Big\lfloor\frac{n-1}{2}\Big\rfloor+\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m+1}{2}\Big\rfloor\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2\Big\lfloor\frac{m-1}{3}\Big\rfloor+\frac{1}{2}(m-1+\mathbf{1}_{m\not\equiv n\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{n\equiv 0\bmod 2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2\Big\lfloor\frac{m-1}{3}\Big\rfloor+\frac{1}{2}(m-\mathbf{1}_{m\equiv n\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{n\equiv 0\bmod 2}).

The computations for
s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}
and
s_{0}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}
are completely analogous. Similarly, since
N_{0}(m)+N_{1}(m)+N_{2}(m)=m(m-1)/2
we can write

\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{1}s_{0}}=\frac{m^{2}}{2}+n(n-m)+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{m\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}-N_{2}(m).

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{s_{1}s_{0}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{1}\gamma}}_{s_{1}s_{0}}=

\displaystyle\ \frac{m^{2}-(3n-m)^{2}}{2}+n(3n-2m)+\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{1}_{m\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{m\not\equiv n\bmod 2})

\displaystyle-\Big\lfloor\frac{m-2}{3}\Big\rfloor+\frac{1}{2}n(3n-2m+1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}(n+\mathbf{1}_{m\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{m\not\equiv n\bmod 2})-\Big\lfloor\frac{m-2}{3}\Big\rfloor.

The computation for
s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}
and
s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}
is completely analogous. Finally, since
\frac{m(m-1)}{2}
,
n(n-m)
, and
\lceil\frac{m}{2}\rceil
are all invariant under
s_{2}
, we have:

\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{2}\gamma}}_{w_{s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}}}=\frac{1}{2}\Big(-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lceil\frac{n}{2}\Big\rceil+\Big\lfloor\frac{-n}{2}\Big\rfloor+\Big\lceil\frac{m-n}{2}\Big\rceil\Big)=-\Big\lfloor\frac{n-m}{2}\Big\rfloor-\Big\lceil\frac{n}{2}\Big\rceil.

The computation for
s_{2}s_{1}s_{2}s_{1}s_{0}
is completely analogous.
∎

8.
Characters of
L(\Lambda)
for certain singular
\Lambda

We are now ready to compute characters of certain irreducible representations of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
:

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
w\in c^{0}_{\mathrm{subreg}}
and
i\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mu(w)\in\widehat{S}
. Pick
\lambda=-\Lambda_{i}+\sum_{k\neq i}m_{k}\Lambda_{k}
, and set
\Lambda:=w^{-1}\circ\lambda
. We have

\widehat{R}\operatorname{ch}L(\Lambda)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\gamma\in Q^{\vee}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(uw)({\mathbf{m}}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w}-{\mathbf{m}}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}_{w})e^{ut_{-\gamma}(\lambda+\widehat{\rho})},

where
{\mathbf{m}}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w}-{\mathbf{m}}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}_{w}
are given in Corollaries
7.6
,
7.9
,
7.14
,
7.20
.

Proof.

In general, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{R}\operatorname{ch}L(\Lambda)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\gamma\in Q^{\vee}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(uw){\mathbf{m}}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w}e^{ut_{-\gamma}(\lambda+\widehat{\rho})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\gamma\in Q^{\vee}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(uw){\mathbf{m}}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w}e^{ut_{-\gamma}(\lambda+\widehat{\rho})}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\gamma\in Q^{\vee}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(uw){\mathbf{m}}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w}e^{us_{i}t_{-\gamma}(\lambda+\widehat{\rho})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\gamma\in Q^{\vee}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(uw){\mathbf{m}}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w}e^{ut_{-\gamma}(\lambda+\widehat{\rho})}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\gamma\in Q^{\vee}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(uw){\mathbf{m}}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}_{w}e^{ut_{-\gamma}(\lambda+\widehat{\rho})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\gamma\in Q^{\vee}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(uw)({\mathbf{m}}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w}-{\mathbf{m}}^{w_{s_{i}\gamma}}_{w})e^{ut_{-\gamma}(\lambda+\widehat{\rho})}.\qed

Example 8.2
.

Assume that
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sp}_{2n}
, and
\Lambda=-\Lambda_{0}
. Note that Corollary
7.9
can be re-written as:

\mathbf{m}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}}-\mathbf{m}^{w_{s_{0}\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $\langle\gamma,\overline{\Lambda}_{1}\rangle\geq 1$ and $\langle\gamma,\overline{\Lambda}_{n}\rangle$ is odd}\\
-1&\text{if $\langle\gamma,\overline{\Lambda}_{1}\rangle\leq 0$ and $\langle\gamma,\overline{\Lambda}_{n}\rangle$ is even}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Thus by Theorem
8.1
for
w=s_{0}=w_{\epsilon_{1}}
(substituting
-\gamma
for
\gamma
),

\displaystyle\widehat{R}\operatorname{ch}L(\Lambda)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\gamma\in Q^{\vee}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(u)({\mathbf{m}}^{w_{-\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}}-{\mathbf{m}}^{w_{-s_{0}\gamma}}_{w_{\epsilon_{1}}})e^{ut_{\gamma}(-\Lambda_{0}+\widehat{\rho})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\langle\gamma,\bar{\Lambda}_{1}\rangle\geqslant 0\\
\langle\gamma,\bar{\Lambda}_{n}\rangle\in 2\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(u)e^{ut_{\gamma}(-\Lambda_{0}+\widehat{\rho})}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\langle\gamma,\bar{\Lambda}_{1}\rangle\leqslant-1\\
\langle\gamma,\bar{\Lambda}_{n}\rangle\in 2\mathbb{Z}+1\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(u)e^{ut_{\gamma}(-\Lambda_{0}+\widehat{\rho})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\langle\gamma,\bar{\Lambda}_{1}\rangle\geqslant 0\\
\langle\gamma,\bar{\Lambda}_{n}\rangle\in 2\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(u)e^{ut_{\gamma}(-\Lambda_{0}+\widehat{\rho})}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\langle\gamma,\bar{\Lambda}_{1}\rangle\geqslant 0\\
\langle\gamma,\bar{\Lambda}_{n}\rangle\in 2\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(u)e^{ut_{s_{\theta^{\vee}}(\gamma)-\theta^{\vee}}(-\Lambda_{0}+\widehat{\rho})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\langle\gamma,\bar{\Lambda}_{1}\rangle\geq 0,\,\langle\gamma,\bar{\Lambda}_{n}\rangle\in 2\mathbb{Z}}\epsilon(u)e^{ut_{\gamma}(-\Lambda_{0}+\widehat{\rho})}.

The same formula was obtained in
[
KW18
, Theorem 2.2 (a)]
using different methods.

Example 8.3
.

Assume
\mathfrak{g}=G_{2}
, and
\Lambda=-\Lambda_{0}
. We then have

\widehat{R}\operatorname{ch}L(-\Lambda_{0})=-\sum_{\gamma=m\alpha^{\vee}+n\beta^{\vee}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(u)\Big(\Big\lfloor\frac{m-1}{3}\Big\rfloor+\frac{1}{4}\big(m-\mathbf{1}_{m\equiv n\bmod 2}-\mathbf{1}_{n\equiv 0\bmod 2}\big)\Big)e^{ut_{-\gamma}(-\Lambda_{0}+\widehat{\rho})}.

Example 8.4
.

Assume
\mathfrak{g}=B_{3}
and
\Lambda=-2\Lambda_{0}
. We then have

\widehat{R}\operatorname{ch}L(-2\Lambda_{0})=-\sum_{\gamma=a_{1}\alpha_{1}^{\vee}+a_{2}\alpha_{2}^{\vee}+b\beta^{\vee}}\sum_{u\in W}\epsilon(u)\bigg(\frac{1}{4}(a_{1}-1)-\frac{1}{4}(-1)^{a_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{a_{2}\not\equiv 0\bmod 2}\bigg)e^{ut_{-\gamma}(-2\Lambda_{0}+\widehat{\rho})}.

Appendix A
The Equivariant Derived McKay correspondence

Let
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{SL}}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
be a finite subgroup of order
m
, and let
\tau
be the tautological
2
-dimensional representation of
\Gamma
. Let
(\mathbf{A}^{2})^{[m]}
be the Hilbert scheme of
0
-dimensional subschemes
\xi\subset\mathbf{A}^{2}
of length
m
, i.e.,

(\mathbf{A}^{2})^{[m]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{I\subset{\mathbb{C}}[x,y]:{\mathbb{C}}[x,y]/I\text{ is $m$-dimensional}\}.

Let
\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma
be the closed subscheme

\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{I\subset{\mathbb{C}}[x,y]:I\text{ is $\Gamma$-invariant, and ${\mathbb{C}}[x,y]/I\simeq{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]$ as $\Gamma$-representations}\}.

Then
\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{2}/\Gamma
is a resolution of singularities.
Let
\mathcal{K}
be the tautological
\Gamma
-equivariant vector bundle over
\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma
, with fiber over
\xi\subset X
being
\Gamma(\xi,{\mathcal{O}}_{\xi})
.

Proposition A.1
.

(
[
KV00
]
)
Vector bundle
\mathcal{K}
is a tilting generator, the structure sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma}
is a direct summand in
\mathcal{K}
, and
\mathcal{K}
is globally generated. We have
\operatorname{End}(\mathcal{K})=\mathbb{C}[\mathbf{A}^{2}]\#\Gamma
.

Proof.

Authors of
[
KV00
]
construct the equivalence
\Psi\colon{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{Coh}(\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma))\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}}}\,{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{Coh}_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{A}^{2}))
. It is easy to see (using
[
KV00
, Proposition 1.5]
) that
\Psi(\mathcal{K})=\mathbb{C}[\mathbf{A}^{2}]\#\Gamma
. It then follows from
[
KV00
, Proposition 1.5]
that
\mathcal{K}
is tilting and
\operatorname{End}(\mathcal{K})=\mathbb{C}[\mathbf{A}^{2}]\#\Gamma
. To see that the structure sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma}
is a direct summand in
\mathcal{K}
one can, for example, use the “quiver” realization of
\mathcal{K}
described in Section
A.1
. Finally,
\mathcal{K}
is globally generated as it is the quotient of
\mathbb{C}[x,y]\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma}
(here we consider
\mathbb{C}[x,y]
as an infinite-dimensional vector space over
\mathbb{C}
, compare with the proof of
[
KV00
, Lemma 2.1]
).
∎

It follows from Proposition
A.1
that there is a derived equivalence (cf.
[
KV00
]
)

(77)

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}\big(\!\operatorname{Coh}(\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma)\big)\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}}}\,{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}\big(\!\operatorname{Coh}_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{A}^{2})\big):\mathcal{F}\mapstochar\rightarrow\operatorname{RHom}(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}).

This equivalence is called
derived McKay equivalence
.

A.1.
In the language of quivers

We re-cast the equivalence in the language of quivers, where we now assume
\Gamma\neq 1
to avoid technicalities. Let
M(\Gamma)
be the McKay quiver, i.e., with vertex set
\widehat{I}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{Irr}(\Gamma)
(where for
i\in\widehat{I}
we let
\pi_{i}
denote the corresponding representation of
\Gamma
), and an edge between
i,j\in\widehat{I}
, denoted
i\sim j
, when
\pi_{i}
appears in
\pi_{j}\otimes\tau
. For each
i\in\widehat{I}
, let
d_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\dim(\pi_{i})
.

Recall
[
Wan02
]
that
\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma
admits a quiver description: there is an isomorphism

(78)

\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma\simeq\big\{f\colon\tau\rightarrow_{\Gamma}\operatorname{End}({\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]),v\in{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]:[f(\tau),f(\tau)]=0,\{f(\tau)^{n}v\}_{n\geqslant 0}\text{ generates }{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]\big\}/{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]^{\times},

where
f\colon\tau\rightarrow_{\Gamma}\operatorname{End}({\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma])
means
f
is a
\Gamma
-equivariant homomorphism
\tau\rightarrow\operatorname{End}({\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma])
. It can equivalently be considered a homomorphism
f\colon{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]\otimes\tau\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]
, and the condition that
[f(\tau),f(\tau)]=0
becomes equivalent to the commutativity of

(79)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]\otimes\tau\otimes\tau}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]\otimes\tau\otimes\tau}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]\otimes\tau}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]\otimes\tau}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma].}
swap
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\otimes\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\otimes\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Now, there is an isomorphism:

\hom_{\Gamma}({\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma]\otimes\tau,{\mathbb{C}}[\Gamma])\simeq\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j)\in I\times I\\
i\sim j\end{subarray}}\hom({\mathbb{C}}^{d_{i}},{\mathbb{C}}^{d_{j}}),

which allows us to re-write (
78
) is isomorphic to:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(M(\Gamma))\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Big\{

\displaystyle(x_{ij})\in\prod_{i\sim j}\hom({\mathbb{C}}^{d_{i}},{\mathbb{C}}^{d_{j}}):\text{ for all $i\in\widehat{I}$, }\sum_{j\sim i}x_{ij}x_{ji}=0,

\displaystyle\text{there are no subspaces $0\neq V_{i}\subsetneq{\mathbb{C}}^{d_{i}}$ such that }x_{ij}V_{i}\subset V_{j}\Big\}/\prod_{i\in\widehat{I}}{\mathrm{GL}}_{d_{i}}({\mathbb{C}}),

which notably only depends on the quiver
M(\Gamma)
. Here
\sum_{j\sim i}x_{ij}x_{ji}=0
is equivalent to the commutativity of diagram (
79
). Now the quiver variety carries a tautological bundle of quiver representations
({\mathcal{V}}_{i})_{i\in\widehat{I}}
of dimension
(d_{i})_{i\in\widehat{I}}
, with homomorphisms
x_{ij}\colon{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\rightarrow{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
. The tautological vector bundle
\mathcal{K}
on
\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma
becomes
\bigoplus_{i\in\widehat{I}}\operatorname{End}({\mathcal{V}}_{i})
.

Moreover, as in
[
KV00
, §3.4]
there is an equivalence

\operatorname{Coh}_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{A}^{2})\simeq\operatorname{Rep}\big(M(\Gamma)\big),

where
\operatorname{Rep}(M(\Gamma))
is the category of double quiver representations, i.e., a family of vector spaces
(V_{i})_{i\in\widehat{I}}
and for any edge
(i,j)
a homomorphism
x_{ij}\colon V_{i}\rightarrow V_{j}
such that for any vertex
i\in\widehat{I}
,
\sum_{j\sim i}x_{ji}x_{ij}=0
. Now, (
77
) can be re-formulated as the equivalence:

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}\big(\!\operatorname{Coh}(\mathcal{Q}(M))\big)\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}\big(\!\operatorname{Rep}(M)\big):\mathcal{F}\rightarrow(\hom({\mathcal{V}}_{i},\mathcal{F}))_{i},(-\circ x_{ij})_{i\sim j}

where
M
is a quiver which is an affine Dynkin diagram of type ADE.

Let
G
be a simple Lie group and let
e\in\mathfrak{g}
be a subregular nilpotent. The corresponding Slodowy variety
\widetilde{S}
is isomorphic to
\mathbf{A}^{2}/\!/\Gamma
. It follows from the above that
\mathcal{E}
is
{\mathrm{Z}}_{G}(\varphi)
-equivariant so the McKay equivalence (
77
) can be upgraded to the
equivariant derived McKay equivalence
.

Theorem A.2
.

There is an equivalence

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{Coh}^{{\mathrm{Z}}(e)}(\widetilde{S}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{b}}(\operatorname{Rep}^{{\mathrm{Z}}(e)}(M)),

where
M
is the affine Dynkin quiver of the unfolding of
G^{\vee}
.

Proof.

The same argument as in the proof of
[
BM13
, Proposition 5.2.1]
works.
∎

Remark A.3
.

It follows from Proposition
4.5
together with Proposition
A.1
that the standard
t
-structure on the right-hand side transfers to the exotic
t
-structure on the left-hand side.

Appendix B
Very distinguished nilpotents and quasi-polynomial multiplicities

Roman Bezrukavnikov, Vasily Krylov, and Kenta Suzuki

We first recall the following definition.

Definition B.1
.

An element
e\in\mathcal{N}
is called
distinguished
if
it is not contained in a proper Levi subalgebra.
An element
e\in\mathcal{N}
is called
very distinguished
if every element
e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}
such that
e\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{e^{\prime}}

is distinguished.

The group
{\mathrm{Z}}_{e}

is finite if
e
is distinguished. One example of a very distinguished nilpotent
e
is a subregular nilpotent in types
D
,
E
. It was shown in
[
BKK24
, Equation (31)]
that for such
e
and
w_{i}\in c
(notations of loc. cit.), we have

{\bf{m}}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{i}}=\Big\langle\Lambda_{i},-\gamma+\frac{\|\gamma\|^{2}}{2}K\Big\rangle.

It follows that
\gamma\mapstochar\rightarrow{\bf{m}}^{w_{\gamma}}_{w_{i}}
is the
quadratic
function on
\gamma
in these cases.

Another example of a very distinguished nilpotent
e
is a subregular nilpotent in types
B,F_{4},G_{2}
. It follows from Theorems
7.4
,
7.13
,
7.19
that the function
\gamma\mapstochar\rightarrow{\bf{m}}_{w_{\nu}}^{w_{\gamma}}
will be a
quasi-polynomial
in these cases.
The goal of this section is to prove that in general, for
w_{\nu}\in c
, corresponding to a very distinguished nilpotent, the function
\gamma\mapstochar\rightarrow{\bf{m}}_{w_{\nu}}^{w_{\gamma}}
is a quasi-polynomial and we can estimate its period and degree.

Remark B.2
.

Note that if
c
does not correspond to a very distinguished nilpotent, then the function
\gamma\mapstochar\rightarrow{\bf{m}}_{w}^{w_{\gamma}}
may not be a quasi-polynomial. This, for example, follows from the computation of the functions
{\bf{m}}_{w_{i}}^{w_{\gamma}},i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, for subregular nilpotent in type
A
case (see
[
BKK24
, Equation (38)]
), see also Theorem
7.7
.

Let
e^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{O}}_{c}
be a distinguished nilpotent. Our goal is to describe the eigenvalues of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{N}}}}(\gamma)
, acting on the finite dimensional
vector space
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}})
. We will see that they are roots of unity of order equal to the order of an element
in
{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}
. We will also estimate the sizes of the corresponding Jordan blocks. Our main technical tool is the localization theorem in
K
-theory.

We start with the following general lemma. Let
H
be an algebraic group acting on a variety
X
. Abusing notations, from now on we will denote by
K_{H}(X)
the
complexified

H
-equivariant
K
-group of
X
.

Pick a semisimple element
\zeta\in H
and set
Z:=Z_{H}(\zeta)
. Let
\gamma\subset H
be the conjugacy class of the element
\zeta
. Let
i\colon X^{\zeta}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of the fixed point locus of
\zeta
. In
[
EG08
, Theorem 4.3]
the authors construct the isomorphism of specializations of (complexified)
K
-groups:

i_{!}\colon K_{Z}(X^{\zeta})_{\zeta}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}}}\,K_{H}(X)_{\gamma}.

Lemma B.3
.

If
\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle on
X
then
[\mathcal{L}]\otimes i_{!}x=i_{!}([i^{*}\mathcal{L}]\otimes x)
for every
x\in K_{Z}(X^{\zeta})_{\zeta}
.

Proof.

Step
1
. Assume first that
X
is smooth. Then the claim follows from
[
EG08
, Theorem (b)]
together with the fact that
i^{!}([\mathcal{L}]\otimes y)=[i^{*}\mathcal{L}]\otimes i^{!}y
, where
i^{!}
(as in
[
EG08
, Section 4.2]
) is the composition of the restriction map and
i^{*}
.

Step
2
. In general, as in
[
EG08
, Section 6.1]
consider an equivariant envelope
p\colon\widetilde{X}\rightarrow X
and let
q\colon\widetilde{X}^{\zeta}\rightarrow X^{\zeta}
be the restriction of
p
to the fixed points of
\zeta
.
It follows from
[
EG08
, Theorem 4.3]
that
i_{!}q_{*}=p_{*}\widetilde{i}_{!}
. We then have for every
\widetilde{x}\in K_{Z}(\widetilde{X}^{\zeta})_{\zeta}
(using the projection formula together with Step
1
):

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L}]\otimes i_{!}(q_{*}\widetilde{x})

\displaystyle=[\mathcal{L}]\otimes p_{*}\widetilde{i}_{!}\widetilde{x}=p_{*}(\widetilde{i}_{!}([\widetilde{i}^{*}p^{*}\mathcal{L}]\otimes\widetilde{x}))

\displaystyle=p_{*}(\widetilde{i}([q^{*}i^{*}\mathcal{L}]\otimes\widetilde{x}))=i_{!}q_{*}([q^{*}i^{*}\mathcal{L}]\otimes\widetilde{x})=i_{!}([i^{*}\mathcal{L}]\otimes q_{*}\widetilde{x}).\qed

Surjectivity of
q_{*}
(see
[
EG08
, Proposition 6.7]
) finishes the proof.

We are now ready to describe generalized eigenvalues and eigenspaces of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}}(\gamma)
acting on
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}})
.
To each conjugacy class
[\zeta]\in{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}/_{\operatorname{ad}}{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}
and a connected component
D\subset{\mathscr{B}}^{\zeta}_{e^{\prime}}
, we associate the number
\chi_{e^{\prime},\zeta,D}
by which
\zeta
acts on
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}}(\gamma)|_{D}
. Note that
\chi_{e^{\prime},\zeta,D}^{l}=1
if
\zeta^{l}=1
. Let
{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)\subset{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}
be the centralizer of
\zeta
in
{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}
.

Lemma B.4
.

We have the decomposition

K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}})=\bigoplus_{[\zeta]\in{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}/_{\operatorname{ad}}{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}},D\subset{\mathscr{B}}^{\zeta}_{e^{\prime}}}K(D)^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}
.
After this identification, the action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{N}}}}(\gamma)
on
K(D)^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}
is given by
\chi_{e^{\prime},\gamma,D}\cdot({\mathcal{O}}_{D}(\gamma)\otimes-)
. In particular,
K(D)^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}
lies in the generalized eigenspace of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{N}}}}(\gamma)\otimes-
with eigenvalue
\chi_{e^{\prime},\gamma,D}
, and sizes of the corresponding Jordan blocks do not exceed
\dim D+1\leqslant\dim{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta}+1
.

Proof.

The localization theorem for finite groups (see, for example,
[
EG08
, Theorem 4.3]
) implies

(80)

K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}})=\bigoplus_{[\zeta]\in{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}/_{\operatorname{ad}}{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}}K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}})_{[\zeta]}=\bigoplus_{[\zeta]\in{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}/_{\operatorname{ad}}{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}}K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta})_{\zeta}=\bigoplus_{[\zeta]\in{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}/_{\operatorname{ad}}{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}}K({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta})^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}.

The first identification is clear (use that
{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}
is finite), the second identification is given by
i_{!}
, where
i\colon{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}
is the embedding.
In the last identification we use the isomorphism
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta})_{\zeta}\simeq K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta})_{1}
that can be described as follows (see
[
EG08
, Section 5.2]
): starting from a
{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)
-equivariant sheaf
\mathcal{F}
, the action of
\zeta
induces the decomposition
\mathcal{F}=\bigoplus_{\chi\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}}\mathcal{F}_{\chi}
and the identification above sends
[\mathcal{F}]
to
\sum_{\chi\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}}\chi[\mathcal{F}_{\chi}]
. We also use the identification
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta})_{1}\simeq K({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta})^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}
(which holds as
{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)
is finite).
It now follows from Lemma
B.3
that after the identification (
80
), the action of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{N}}}}(\gamma)
on
K(D)^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}
is given by
\chi_{e^{\prime},\gamma,D}\cdot({\mathcal{O}}_{D}(\gamma)\otimes-)
(twist by
\chi_{e^{\prime},\gamma,D}
appears because of the identification
K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta})_{\zeta}\simeq K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta})_{1}
that we use).
Note now that the operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{D}(\gamma)\otimes-
is unipotent so
\chi_{e^{\prime},\gamma,D}
is the unique eigenvalue of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{N}}}}(\gamma)
restricted to
K(D)^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}
. Moreover,
[{\mathcal{O}}_{D}(\gamma)-{\mathcal{O}}_{D}]\otimes-
is equal to zero being raised to the
(\dim D+1)
-th power (induction on
\dim D
), so all of the Jordan blocks of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}}(\gamma)
acting on
K(D)^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}
do not exceed
\dim D+1
.
∎

For a distinguished nilpotent
e^{\prime}
let
d_{e^{\prime},\chi}
be the maximum of
\dim D+1
, where
D
is a connected component of
{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta}
such that
\chi_{e^{\prime},\zeta,D}=\chi
.

Let
e\in\mathcal{N}
be a very distinguished nilpotent. Set
U\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigsqcup_{e\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{e^{\prime}}}\mathbb{O}_{e^{\prime}}\subset\mathcal{N}
, this is an open
G^{\vee}
-invariant subset of
\mathcal{N}
.

Corollary B.5
.

Decomposition into generalized eigenspaces of
Q^{\vee}
acting on
K^{G^{\vee}}\!({\widetilde{U}})
can be described as follows: possible eigenvalues
\chi
are
\chi_{e^{\prime},\zeta,D}
for some
e^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{e}
,
\zeta\in{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}
,
D\subset{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta}
. Dimension of the corresponding eigenspace is equal to
\sum_{e^{\prime},\zeta,D}\dim K(D)^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}(\zeta)}
, where the sum runs over all
e^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{e}
,
[\zeta]\in{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}/_{\operatorname{ad}}{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}
,
D\subset{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta}
such that
\chi_{e^{\prime},\zeta,D}=\chi
. The sizes of the Jordan normal blocks corresponding to
\chi
do not exceed
d_{U,\chi}:=\sum_{e\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{e^{\prime}}}d_{e^{\prime},\chi}
.

Proof.

Note that there exists a filtration on the
Q^{\vee}
-module
K^{G^{\vee}}\!({\widetilde{U}})
labeled by
\mathbb{O}_{e^{\prime}}
such that
e\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{e^{\prime}}
and with associated graded being isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{e^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{e}}K^{{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}}({\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}})
(see
[
BKK24
, Section 4.2]
). Claim follows from Lemma
B.4
.
∎

We identify
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\oplus r}\,\vphantom{j^{X^{2}}}\smash{\overset{\sim}{\vphantom{\rule{0.0pt}{1.99997pt}}\smash{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}}}\,Q^{\vee}
via
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{r})\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{i=1}^{r}a_{i}\alpha_{i}^{\vee}
.

Definition B.6
.

A function
f\colon Q^{\vee}={\mathbb{Z}}^{\oplus r}\rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}
is a
quasi-polynomial with period
l
if

f(a_{1},\ldots,a_{r})=\sum_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{r}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}}c_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{r}}\!(a_{1},\ldots,a_{r})a_{1}^{d_{1}}\ldots a_{r}^{d_{r}},

where
c_{d_{1},\ldots,d_{r}}
are almost all equal to zero and descend to the functions on
({\mathbb{Z}}/l{\mathbb{Z}})^{\oplus r}
.
A function
{\mathbf{m}}\colon Q^{\vee}\rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}
is a quasi-polynomial if it is a quasi-polynomial with period
l
for some
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
.

Assume that the group
Q^{\vee}
acts on a finite dimensional vector space
V
. Assume also that there exists a character
\chi\colon Q^{\vee}\rightarrow\mu_{l}
such that the operator
(\gamma-\chi(\gamma))^{p}=0
for some
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}
.

Lemma B.7
.

For every
v\in V
,
u\in V^{*}
, the function
Q^{\vee}\ni\gamma\mapstochar\rightarrow\langle u,\gamma v\rangle
is a quasi-polynomial with period
l
and degree
<p
.

Proof.

Pick a basis
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}
of
V
in which
Q^{\vee}
acts via upper triangular operators. Recall the identification
Q^{\vee}={\mathbb{Z}}^{\oplus r}
, for
k\in\{1,\ldots,r\}
let
A_{k}
be the operator by which
\alpha_{k}^{\vee}
acts on
V
. The matrix of
A_{k}
in the basis
v_{i}
has the following form:

A_{k}=\begin{pmatrix}\chi(\alpha_{k}^{\vee})&*&\ldots&*\\
0&\chi(\alpha_{k}^{\vee})&\ldots&*\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&*\\
0&0&\ldots&\chi(\alpha_{k}^{\vee})\end{pmatrix}=\chi(\alpha_{k}^{\vee})\begin{pmatrix}1&*&\ldots&*\\
0&1&\ldots&*\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&*\\
0&0&\ldots&1\end{pmatrix}.

It remains to check that the entries of the matrix
A_{1}^{a_{1}}\ldots A_{r}^{a_{r}}
are quasi-polynomials with period
l
and degree
<p
.
Set
B_{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{1}{\chi(\alpha_{k}^{\vee})}A_{k}
. Since
\chi(\alpha_{k}^{\vee})^{l}=1
we see that it is enough to show that the entries of the matrix
B_{1}^{a_{1}}\ldots B_{r}^{a_{r}}
are polynomials of degree
<p
. This is standard.
∎

Theorem B.8
.

Suppose that
w_{\nu}\in c
and
{\mathbb{O}}_{c}
consists of very distinguished elements.
Then the function
\gamma\mapstochar\rightarrow{\bf{m}}_{w_{\nu}}^{w_{\gamma}}
is a quasi-polynomial. It has the form

{\bf{m}}_{w_{\nu}}^{w_{\gamma}}=\sum_{\chi}f_{\chi}(\gamma),

where
f_{\chi}(\gamma)
is a quasi-polynomial with period
l=\mathrm{order}(\chi)
, degree of
f
is at most
d_{U,\chi}-1
.

Proof.

Note that
{\bf{m}}_{w_{\nu}}^{w_{\gamma}}
is a matrix coefficient of the operator
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{N}}}}(\gamma)\otimes-
, acting on
K^{G^{\vee}}\!(\widetilde{U})
.
The statement now follows from Corollary
B.5
and Lemma
B.7
. ∎

Remark B.9
.

Recall that the set of
\chi
appearing in Theorem
B.8
runs through all
\chi_{e^{\prime},\zeta,D}
, where
e^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{e}
,
\zeta\in{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}
,
D\subset{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta}
, and
\chi_{e^{\prime},\zeta,D}
is the number by which
\zeta
acts on
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}}(\gamma)|_{D}
. Recall also that
d_{U,\chi}=\sum_{e\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}_{e^{\prime}}}d_{e^{\prime},\chi}
with
d_{e^{\prime},\chi}
being the maximum of
\dim D+1
over all connected components
D\subset{\mathscr{B}}_{e^{\prime}}^{\zeta}
such that
\chi=\chi_{e^{\prime},\zeta,D}
. Lemma
B.4
gives an effective bound
on degrees and periods of the quasi-polynomials
f_{\chi}
(let us recall that the order of
\chi=\chi_{e^{\prime},\zeta,D}
divides the order of
\zeta
that in turn divides
|{\mathrm{Z}}_{e^{\prime}}|
).
These may not be optimal.

Example B.10
.

To apply Theorem
B.8
one needs an effective way of finding
{\mathbb{O}}_{c}

given
w\in c
. While this is not easy in general, recall that the cell
c
such that
{\mathbb{O}}_{c}

is subregular consists of elements
w\neq 1
with a unique minimal decomposition.
A similar (but more complicated) description of the next case, which includes most
very distinguished examples, should follow from
[
GX20
]
.
